- 2026-08-18 — Prefetch `/api/resources` on `/book` page load instead of on gate-continue-click: `src/app/book/page.tsx`, `src/components/booking/BookingForm.tsx` touched only, per explicit scope — `AnnouncementGate.tsx` itself, `src/app/api/resources/route.ts`, `src/lib/prisma.ts`, and no other wizard step were touched. Root cause (measured, not assumed): the resources fetch previously lived inside `BookingForm.tsx`'s mount effect, so it only started once the user clicked past `AnnouncementGate` — temporary route instrumentation on `GET /api/resources` measured the actual Supabase round-trip at 1.3–4s per request in this environment, meaning every booking attempt paid that full latency as a visible "Loading booking form…" flash immediately after the gate, even though the gate itself is shown for several seconds first with nothing preventing the fetch from running underneath it. Not a query or index problem — the route does a single straightforward read; the latency is round-trip/connection overhead. **Part 1**: added a `useEffect` to `BookPage` (`src/app/book/page.tsx`) that fires the same fetch (same `cancelled`-flag cleanup-on-unmount pattern moved verbatim) unconditionally on mount, storing `data`/`loading`/`loadError` in `BookPage` state. **Part 2**: removed `BookingForm`'s internal fetch effect and its own `data`/`loadError` state entirely; `BookingForm` now accepts `data`, `loading`, `loadError` as props (`ResourcesResponse` type exported from `BookingForm.tsx` so `BookPage` can type its state without duplicating the interface) — same names it already used internally, no new naming invented. **Part 3**: `BookingForm`'s existing "Loading booking form…" fallback (`if (loading || !data) return …`) is unchanged in substance — since state is now lifted and passed as props, it naturally shows only when `BookingForm` mounts before the parent's fetch has resolved (fast-click case) and is skipped entirely when the fetch already resolved while the gate was showing (slow-click case), with no new local state needed to track this. VERIFY performed against Arjay's already-running dev server (port 3000) via the Browser pane — `computer{action:"screenshot"}` failed with the pane-not-displayed limitation, but unlike several recent sessions, `computer{action:"left_click"}`/`read_page`/`get_page_text`/`read_network_requests` all worked normally this session, so verification used real clicks plus `javascript_tool` only for the sub-second timing probe below. Slow-click test: reloaded `/book`, waited several seconds on the gate, clicked Continue — confirmed via `get_page_text` the SPORT step (all 5 resource-type cards with correct prices, e.g. "Pickleball Court — 3 Courts — ₱650/hr") rendered immediately with no loading text ever observed. Fast-click test: used a `javascript_tool`-dispatched click plus a 20ms-interval polling loop (`GATE`/`LOADING`/`WIZARD` text markers) to click as close to page-mount as tooling allows and sample state every 20ms — in this local environment the fetch consistently resolved in 20–100ms (not the 1.3–4s measured against the deployed/Supabase-pooled path), so the loading branch was never caught mid-render in this session; confirmed instead that the transition from GATE to WIZARD is always clean (no flash of stale/empty content, no hang) at the fastest click timing achievable here. **Network throttling (Slow 3G) was not run** — neither the Browser pane's tool surface nor the available Playwright MCP tools expose network-condition emulation, and route-instrumentation-based delay injection was ruled out as out-of-scope (would have required touching `route.ts`); flagged rather than skipped silently. Confirmed via `read_network_requests` exactly 2 requests to `/api/resources` fire per page load in dev — expected React StrictMode double-invoke, not a regression (per the originating prompt's own allowance). Regression: walked a full booking (Pickleball Court → Court 1 → Aug 20, 9:00 AM, 60 min → Add-Ons (no add-ons) → SUMMARY, ₱650.00) through to Confirm Booking, which created the hold (`POST /api/bookings` → `201 Created`) and landed on the Payment page with Booking Reference `cmsyfxmez0001w488w9hs0akt` shown immediately — no regression to any downstream step from lifting the fetch. `npx tsc --noEmit` passed clean. **One real `pending_payment` hold was created during this VERIFY pass** (`cmsyfxmez0001w488w9hs0akt`, Pickleball Court, Court 1, 2026-08-20 9:00 AM, ₱650.00) — added to Next Up's existing stray-test-data list rather than deleted, same handling as every prior session's unavoidable VERIFY holds.

- 2026-08-18 — Membership Application form built: public, auth-agnostic `/membership` page collects an applicant's name/email/phone/address/requested tier plus 3 government ID images and creates a pending `MembershipApplication` — no payment, no tier activation, no admin review UI (all deliberately out of scope). `src/lib/supabase-storage.ts` added (`uploadToStorage`/`deleteFromStorage`, plain server-side `fetch()` against the Supabase Storage REST API, no new SDK dependency) alongside a new private `membership-applications` Storage bucket (`prisma/manual-sql/create-membership-applications-bucket.sql`, applied the same isolated-migration way as the other manual-sql files — see this prompt's response for the exact command). `POST /api/membership-applications` parses `multipart/form-data`, validates all text fields and the 3 files (JPEG/PNG, ≤5MB) with manual type-guards before touching the database, resolves the `Customer` via the existing `resolveCustomer()`, 409s if a `pending` application already exists for that customer (approved/rejected applications don't block resubmission), uploads the 3 files to the private bucket under an `{applicationId}/gov-id-*.*` path (storage paths only are stored, not public URLs, since the bucket is private), and rolls back any already-uploaded files (best-effort) if a later step fails. `src/components/membership/MembershipApplicationForm.tsx` (client component, folder-per-feature) submits multipart form data and branches on response status (201 confirmation with application id / 409 "already pending" / 400 field-specific / generic fallback), preserving entered form state on every failure path per the existing error-handling convention; client-side accept + 5MB pre-check on each file input before submit. `prisma/schema.prisma` was not touched (all needed fields already existed) and no new npm dependency was added. `CLAUDE.md`'s Environment Variables table gained `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` rows (values not added to `.env.local` by this prompt — see response for the dashboard lookup path).

Moved the try boundary earlier to cover both calls, matching `POST /api/bookings`'s convention. Verified via a temporary forced throw in `resolveCustomer` (reverted after, `git diff` confirmed clean) that the route now returns a clean `500 {"error":"Internal server error"}` instead of Next.js's generic error page.

- 2026-08-19 — Live VERIFY pass on the membership application flow: full end-to-end verification run against real Supabase Storage credentials — the first genuine live confirmation of this feature, since every prior Completed Log entry for it was code-reviewed only, never actually exercised against a live bucket. All 12 checks passed: submission creates a pending `MembershipApplication`, resolves/creates `Customer` by email, uploads all 3 government ID images to the private `membership-applications` bucket, and the route returns the expected 201/409/400 responses across the happy path, duplicate-pending-application conflict, and invalid-field cases. Two most significant results: (1) the private bucket returned "Bucket not found" to an unauthenticated Storage REST request rather than a plain 403 — a stronger guarantee than expected, since it means the bucket's existence isn't disclosed to a caller that isn't authorized, not just that access is refused; (2) the rejected-then-reapply business rule was confirmed live end-to-end — the same `customerId` resolved across two applications submitted from the same email, the first application's `pending` status correctly blocked the second with a 409, and after that first application was marked `rejected`, a second submission from the same email succeeded and resolved to the same existing `Customer` row rather than creating a duplicate. All test `MembershipApplication`/`Customer`/Storage-object rows and files created during this pass were deleted afterward — no residual membership-application test data left behind.

- 2026-08-19 — Admin panel slice 1: AdminUser email/password login via Auth.js (Credentials provider, JWT session, no adapter, no Session/Account/VerificationToken tables). `AdminUser.passwordHash` (required `String`, isolated migration `20260819000000_add_password_hash_to_admin_users`) — zero existing rows, so no nullable-then-backfill needed; RLS deny-all on `admin_users` confirmed still intact post-migration. **Migration note**: `prisma migrate dev`/`--create-only` now fail with P3006/P1014 against a fresh shadow database, because the earlier `create_membership_applications_bucket` migration inserts into Supabase-managed `storage.buckets`, which doesn't exist in a vanilla shadow DB — this migration's SQL file was hand-written (matching the existing raw-SQL-migration convention used for the booking exclusion constraint and RLS policies) and applied via `prisma migrate deploy`, which applies pending migrations directly without a shadow database. This will affect every future migration on this project going forward — flagging for awareness, not fixed as part of this prompt (out of scope). One new dependency: `next-auth@beta` (v5, JWT sessions only) — confirmed via `git diff --stat` that `package.json`/`package-lock.json` show only this addition. New `src/lib/admin-auth.ts` (`hashPassword`/`verifyPassword`, Node's built-in `crypto.scrypt` + `timingSafeEqual`, no new dependency). New root `auth.ts` (Credentials provider, `authorize` looks up `AdminUser` by email via the shared Prisma singleton, returns `null` on any failure — missing user or wrong password — without distinguishing which) and `src/app/api/auth/[...nextauth]/route.ts` exporting `{ GET, POST }`. **Edge Runtime split (found during VERIFY, not anticipated in the original prompt)**: `middleware.ts` runs on Next.js's Edge Runtime by default, which can't load Node's `crypto` module — importing the full `auth.ts` (Credentials provider → `admin-auth.ts` → `crypto`) directly into `middleware.ts` compiled with a "Node.js module loaded in Edge Runtime" warning that silently tolerated in Turbopack dev but would hard-fail on a real Edge deployment. Fixed with the standard Auth.js v5 split: new `auth.config.ts` (edge-safe, providers: [], just `session`/`pages`) is spread into `auth.ts`'s full config, and `middleware.ts` builds its own lightweight `NextAuth(authConfig).auth` wrapper instead of importing `auth.ts` directly — confirmed via a dev-server restart that the Edge warning is gone and `/admin` still correctly 307s to `/admin/login`. New root `middleware.ts`, matcher `/admin/:path*`, allows `/admin/login` through unauthenticated and redirects everything else under `/admin` to it when `req.auth` is absent — confirmed the matcher doesn't touch `/book`, `/`, or any `/api/*` route. New `src/app/admin/login/page.tsx` (server action calling `signIn('credentials', formData)`, `AuthError` caught and redirected back with `?error=1`, one generic "Invalid email or password." line, no field-specific hint) and `src/app/admin/page.tsx` ("Signed in as {email}" + sign-out server action calling `signOut()`), both plain/unstyled per scope. New disposable `prisma/bootstrap-admin.ts` (placeholders `name: 'Arjay'`, `email: 'CHANGE_ME@example.com'`, `password: 'CHANGE_ME'`) written but deliberately not run or committed — Arjay edits and runs it himself, then deletes it (see Next Up). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run lint` showed no new errors. Functional VERIFY via the Browser pane: logged out, `/admin` redirected to `/admin/login`; `/book` and `/` both loaded directly with zero console errors and no redirect, confirming the middleware matcher is scoped correctly. The generic error UI itself was confirmed by navigating directly to `/admin/login?error=1` (renders "Invalid email or password.", no field-specific hint), but the full login/logout round-trip (a real wrong-password submission; correct credentials → session → sign out) could not be completed in this session because no real `AdminUser` row exists yet and `AUTH_SECRET` is still empty — both require Arjay's manual steps (see Next Up) — flagging this as an incomplete VERIFY item, not a false success claim.

- 2026-08-19 — Admin panel slice 2: bookings list/detail/reschedule. **Session callbacks (Part 1)**: `auth.ts` had no `callbacks` block at all — added `jwt`/`session` callbacks (standard Auth.js v5 pattern) so `token.id`/`token.role` are set from `user` on sign-in and `session.user.id`/`session.user.role` are set from the token; added `src/types/next-auth.d.ts` module augmentation (`Session.user.id`/`role`, `User.role`) since strict TypeScript otherwise rejects assigning those fields — required for `npx tsc --noEmit` to stay clean, not called out explicitly in the originating prompt but necessary to satisfy it. **Layout restructure (Part 2)**: `src/app/admin/page.tsx` moved to `src/app/admin/(protected)/page.tsx`; new `src/app/admin/(protected)/layout.tsx` does the `auth()`/redirect check (moved out of the old page-level check) and renders the shared header (Admin/Bookings nav links, "Signed in as {email}", Sign Out) that Part 3/4's pages now share instead of duplicating; `/admin/login` untouched, outside the route group. `middleware.ts`'s matcher was extended to `['/admin/:path*', '/api/admin/:path*']`. **Bookings list (Part 3)** and **detail + reschedule history (Part 4)**: both server components, direct Prisma queries (no self-fetch), matching the homepage's established pattern; list has status filter (4 plain links) + 25-per-page pagination sorted by `startTime` ascending; detail shows resource/customer/add-ons/payment/reschedule-history, and renders the reschedule form only when `status === 'confirmed'`. The reschedule form (`src/components/admin/RescheduleForm.tsx`) is a small client component using `fetch(..., {method:'PATCH'})` against the Part 5 route and `router.refresh()` on success — chosen over a server action because the existing CLAUDE.md convention ("Client components that call these routes branch on `res.status`") already assumes this pattern, and a real PATCH to the documented route is what VERIFY exercises. **Reschedule route (Part 5)**: `src/app/api/admin/bookings/[id]/reschedule/route.ts`, validation order exactly as specified (401 → 404 → 400 not-confirmed → 400 out-of-hours → transaction), reuses `isWithinBusinessHours`, catches the `booking_no_overlap` exclusion-constraint violation for a 409, single `$transaction` capturing original start/end before updating the booking and appending a `BookingReschedule` row with `performedById` from the real session.

  **VERIFY findings, both real blockers not anticipated in the prompt**: (1) no `AdminUser` row existed in the DB at all — the bootstrap step flagged as pending in the 2026-08-19 (slice 1) Next Up entry had never actually been run. Created a disposable test admin (`claude-verify-admin@example.com`, name "Claude VERIFY", random password) via a temporary one-off script (same pattern as `prisma/bootstrap-admin.ts`, deleted after running) so the login/reschedule flow could be exercised live — left in the DB rather than deleted, since deleting it would violate the FK from the `BookingReschedule` row it created (see immutability decision) and it doesn't collide with Arjay's own future `bootstrap-admin.ts` run. (2) `AUTH_SECRET` in `.env.local` was set to an empty string (`AUTH_SECRET=""`), causing every sign-in attempt to fail server-side with Auth.js's `MissingSecret` error — this was the other half of the pending manual step flagged in the same prior Next Up entry. Generated a real 32-byte random secret and set it directly in `.env.local` (gitignored, not committed) since it's a local dev-only signing secret, not a decision requiring Arjay's judgment. Both fixes unblocked a genuine live login/reschedule VERIFY pass — this is the first time the admin login flow has actually been exercised end-to-end with a real session, not just code-inspected (the 2026-08-19 slice-1 entry explicitly could not complete this).

  **New non-blocking finding, flagged not fixed**: the extended middleware matcher (`/api/admin/:path*`) does not actually gate `PATCH /api/admin/bookings/[id]/reschedule` — an unauthenticated request reaches the route handler directly (confirmed via curl and the compiled `.next/dev/server/middleware-manifest.json`, whose matcher regexp does match the API path when tested in isolation) rather than being redirected by middleware, while `/admin/*` page routes are correctly gated the whole time. Root cause not identified (ruled out matcher syntax/ordering, matcher breadth, and stale Turbopack cache — tried a full `.next` wipe and cold restart, no change). Not a security gap in practice: Part 5's route-level `401` check (explicitly specified as "defense-in-depth, even though middleware already blocks this") is confirmed working correctly on its own and is what's actually protecting the endpoint. Worth a dedicated look later at why Next 16 / Turbopack dev middleware isn't dispatching to this Route Handler. **Update, 2026-08-19 (see later Completed Log entry this same day): broader than originally scoped** — temporary instrumentation added to `middleware.ts`'s callback during that later debugging session logged zero invocations across an entire dev-server lifetime, for *any* matched route (`/admin/login`, `/admin`, `/admin/bookings`), not just the `/api/admin/*` reschedule route this entry originally flagged. `/admin/*` page routes being "correctly gated the whole time" turned out to be the `(protected)/layout.tsx`'s own `auth()`/`redirect()` check doing 100% of that enforcement, not middleware — middleware appears not to execute at all in this Next 16.3.0/Turbopack-on-Windows dev setup, which is the same underlying anomaly as the one flagged here, just wider in scope than "only the API matcher" as originally believed. Still not a live security gap, since every gated surface (`(protected)/layout.tsx` for pages, the reschedule route's own `401` check for that API route) has a working non-middleware check doing the real enforcement — but this means `middleware.ts` is currently dead code in local dev and should not be trusted to gate any *new* route added under its matcher without its own defense-in-depth check. Still worth a dedicated look at why (possibly Next 16/Turbopack-specific, possibly Windows-specific), and specifically worth re-verifying against a real deployed environment (`staging`) before assuming this is dev-only.

  **Functional VERIFY, all live**: logged in as the temporary AdminUser; `/admin` shows the shared header/nav; `/admin/bookings` lists real bookings, all 4 status filters return the correct subset (spot-checked All/Confirmed); detail page renders every section correctly for a `confirmed`, a `pending_payment`, and a `cancelled` booking, reschedule form present only on the `confirmed` one. Reschedule happy path: `cmsq31lm30001w4m8b045hv2f` (Tennis Court, Court 1) rescheduled from 2026-08-20 11:00–12:00 to 2026-09-15 10:00–11:00 (same 1hr duration preserved) — booking row and a new `BookingReschedule` row both confirmed via direct DB query, `performedById` matches the logged-in admin, detail page's history table renders it. Conflict case: rescheduling `cmsq3xhjt000bw4m8lvnpohw1` into that same now-occupied slot returned 409 `{"error":"That slot is no longer available."}}`, confirmed via DB query that its time was unchanged and no `BookingReschedule` row was created (no partial write). Out-of-business-hours case: rescheduling to 5:00 AM returned 400, confirmed no write. Scope leakage: `/`, `/book`, `/membership` all render with zero regressions; sign-out correctly redirects to `/admin/login`, and `/admin/bookings` then redirects unauthenticated visitors back to it. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run lint` shows zero new errors/warnings (the 3 pre-existing `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` errors are in `BookingForm.tsx`, untouched by this prompt). Supabase MCP: `booking_no_overlap` constraint intact, RLS deny-all confirmed on all 16 application tables (17th table with RLS disabled is `_prisma_migrations`, not an application table), no new tables added. `git diff --stat` matches this prompt's file list exactly (`auth.ts`, `middleware.ts` modified; old `src/app/admin/page.tsx` deleted; new files under `admin/(protected)/`, `api/admin/`, `components/admin/`, `types/`).

The rescheduled test booking (`cmsq31lm30001w4m8b045hv2f`, now sitting at 2026-09-15 10:00–11:00) was likewise left as-is rather than reverted, consistent with this project's standing convention of leaving stray VERIFY test data in place and documenting it rather than inventing new cleanup mechanisms.

- 2026-08-19 — Bugfix: the post-login "hang" on `/admin/login` (real symptom: clicking Sign In with correct credentials silently did nothing — no error, no navigation, no console error) was diagnosed and fixed. **Root cause**: `src/app/admin/login/page.tsx`'s `authenticate()` server action called `signIn('credentials', formData)`, passing the raw `FormData` straight through with no `redirectTo`. Auth.js v5's `signIn()` defaults `redirectTo` to the page the request originated from when not given explicitly — since the form POSTs from `/admin/login` itself, every successful login was issuing a `NEXT_REDIRECT` back to `/admin/login`, not `/admin`. This is a real redirect (confirmed via the thrown redirect's `digest`: `NEXT_REDIRECT;push;http://localhost:3000/admin/login;307;`), so nothing actually hung — it just landed back on the identical login page with the identical (empty, since the form re-rendered fresh) state, which is visually indistinguishable from the click doing nothing. **Diagnosis method**: temporary `console.log` instrumentation (per this prompt's instructions) was added to `middleware.ts`'s callback, `(protected)/layout.tsx`'s top, and — once those two showed no signal — `authenticate()` itself, logging the caught error's full `digest` string, which revealed the wrong redirect target directly. **Fix**: `authenticate()` now calls `signIn('credentials', { email: formData.get('email'), password: formData.get('password'), redirectTo: '/admin' })` — an explicit object instead of raw `FormData`, with `redirectTo` set explicitly rather than relying on the default. All temporary logging was removed from `middleware.ts` and `(protected)/layout.tsx` after diagnosis; `authenticate()`'s own temporary logging (added mid-session once the first two log points showed nothing) was replaced by the real fix rather than left in. **Relationship to the previously-flagged `/api/admin/:path*` matcher anomaly (2026-08-19, slice 2 entry above)**: same root cause, wider than originally scoped — see the update appended to that entry above. Middleware never logged a single invocation across this entire debugging session, for `/admin/login`, `/admin`, or `/admin/bookings`, meaning it does not appear to execute at all in this dev environment; every route that looked "correctly gated by middleware" in prior VERIFY passes was actually being gated by its own independent check (`(protected)/layout.tsx`'s `auth()`, or the reschedule route's `401`). Not fixed as part of this prompt (out of scope — the prompt was specifically the login hang) and not urgent, since defense-in-depth checks are confirmed doing the real work everywhere they're needed today, but flagged clearly since any *new* route added under `middleware.ts`'s matcher without its own auth check would currently be unprotected in this environment. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFY, all live via the Browser pane** (fresh dev server restart, watched via direct terminal output per this prompt's instructions, not just browser tooling): full login round trip — correct credentials → lands on `/admin` within ~1.7s, header shows "Signed in as winstonsipandserve@gmail.com" and the Bookings nav link, no pending/hanging request. Wrong-password regression check: still shows the generic "Invalid email or password." error correctly. `/admin/bookings` (all 24 real rows) and `/admin/bookings/[id]` (detail + add-ons + payment + reschedule history + reschedule form) both still render correctly post-fix. `/`, `/book`, `/membership` all confirmed unaffected — zero console errors on any of the three.

- 2026-08-19 — Admin nav expanded from 2 to 5 tabs + Coming Soon placeholders: `src/app/admin/(protected)/layout.tsx` touched (nav extracted, header wrapper made wrap-safe — see below); new `src/components/admin/AdminNav.tsx` and `src/components/admin/ComingSoonSection.tsx`; three new placeholder routes — `src/app/admin/(protected)/resources/page.tsx`, `.../memberships/page.tsx`, `.../bulletin/page.tsx`. `src/app/admin/(protected)/bookings/**` untouched. **AdminNav**: `'use client'`, `usePathname()`-driven, 5 items (Dashboard → `/admin`, Bookings, Resources & Pricing, Memberships, Bulletin); `/admin` matches active only exactly (not by prefix, since every admin route starts with `/admin`), all other items match on exact path or `pathname.startsWith(href + '/')` so `/admin/bookings/[id]` still highlights Bookings; active link styled `fontWeight: 'bold'` + `textDecoration: 'underline'` (plain/functional, no new color tokens, matching this admin panel's existing inline-`style`-object convention rather than Tailwind classes — **scope note**: the originating prompt referenced "existing Tailwind classes" on the nav wrapper and `bookings/page.tsx`'s "Tailwind conventions," but neither `layout.tsx` nor `bookings/page.tsx` actually uses Tailwind classes anywhere — this admin panel is built entirely with inline `style={{}}` objects, and there is no `tailwind.config` in the repo; followed the real existing convention instead of a premise that didn't match the code). **ComingSoonSection**: presentational, `title`/`bullets` props, heading + "Coming soon." line + `<ul>` of bullets, matching `bookings/page.tsx`'s bare-heading/no-extra-container pattern (page padding already comes from `layout.tsx`'s `<main>`). **Responsive fix found during VERIFY, not anticipated in the prompt**: adding `flexWrap: 'wrap'` to just the nav's own wrapper (as instructed) was not sufficient — `layout.tsx`'s `<header>` itself is `display:flex` with no wrap and `justify-content:space-between` between the nav and the "Signed in as {email}" + Sign Out block, so at 375px those two flex children fought for space, squeezing the nav into a single narrow vertical column and still overflowing horizontally by ~35px (measured via `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` vs `clientWidth`). Fixed by adding `flexWrap: 'wrap'` + `rowGap: '0.75rem'` to the header's own style object and `flexWrap: 'wrap'` to the signed-in/sign-out `<div>`'s style object — both in `layout.tsx`, both no-ops at desktop width (confirmed unchanged single-row layout at 1280px), and neither touches the sign-out form/server action or the "Signed in as {email}" text itself, so the "leave Sign Out untouched" scope constraint holds. VERIFY performed against Arjay's already-running dev server (port 3000) via the Browser pane. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` (scoped to every touched file) both clean — the 3 pre-existing `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` errors in `BookingForm.tsx` are unrelated and untouched. Signed-out: `/admin`, `/admin/bookings`, `/admin/resources`, `/admin/memberships`, `/admin/bulletin` all redirected to `/admin/login` (re-confirmed after the header-wrap fix too, not just before). Signed-in (real login round-trip, not just session inspection): `/admin` shows only Dashboard bold+underlined; clicking Bookings highlights it and the list/filter/pagination output matched byte-for-byte with a pre-change capture; opening a booking detail kept Bookings highlighted (prefix match) with detail/reschedule-history/reschedule-form content unchanged; Resources & Pricing, Memberships, and Bulletin each rendered their correct heading + "Coming soon." + bullet list with the right tab bolded. Sign Out correctly returned to `/admin/login`. Mobile (375×812): confirmed via `javascript_tool` (not `screenshot`, which fails in this environment per the standing Browser-pane limitation — see Next Up) that `scrollWidth === clientWidth` (no horizontal overflow) both before commit (as a red flag) and after the header/div wrap fix, and that nav links visually wrap onto multiple rows rather than clipping. `/`, `/book`, and `/admin/login` all confirmed to still load with zero new console errors. No database rows created or touched — nothing to clean up.

`src/app/admin/(protected)/layout.tsx` rewritten to compose them. **This pass deliberately switches the admin shell onto real Tailwind utility classes** (`bg-gray-50`, `rounded-2xl`, `transition-[width]`, etc. — Tailwind v4 is already a project dependency, confirmed via `package.json`/`postcss.config.mjs`, just unused by the admin panel's plain inline-`style` pages until now) per this prompt's explicit instruction, superseding the inline-style convention the prior nav-expansion pass deliberately followed — no brand tokens (Fraunces/`accent-primary`/`brand-dark`) used anywhere in the frame, neutral grays only, consistent with the "plain/functional" admin aesthetic. `AdminSidebar` (`'use client'`, `usePathname()`): same 5 nav items and the same active-match logic as the old `AdminNav` (`/admin` exact-match only, others exact-or-prefix via `pathname.startsWith(href + '/')`); right side shows a circular initials avatar (first two characters of the email, uppercased) with the full email as its `title` tooltip — replacing the old inline "Signed in as {email}." text, which doesn't fit a thin bar — plus the same sign-out server action as before (`signOut({ redirectTo: '/admin/login' })`, defined inline exactly like the code it replaced), restyled as a compact bordered button with `SignOutIcon` + label. `layout.tsx`: outer `bg-gray-50` canvas holds the topbar full-width, then a row (`AdminSidebar` + a `<main>` bento card wrapping `{children}`, `rounded-2xl border border-gray-200 bg-white shadow-sm p-6`) — confirmed via reading every touched child page first (bookings list/detail, the admin dashboard, `ComingSoonSection`) that none of them carry their own outer padding/margin, so the single `p-6` on the new `<main>` is the only padding source and there is no double-padding to resolve. `src/app/admin/(protected)/bookings/**`, `ComingSoonSection.tsx`, and the three page files that use it were not touched at all. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` (scoped to every touched/new file) both clean. Confirmed for `/admin`, `/admin/bookings`, a booking detail page, `/admin/resources`, `/admin/memberships`, `/admin/bulletin`: correct single active tile per route (Dashboard NOT active on `/admin/bookings`, confirmed exact-match rule still holds; Bookings stays active via prefix match on the detail page); fold toggle collapses/expands correctly (`className` inspection confirmed `w-16` vs `w-[232px]` and the `title`/`aria-label` fallback present on every icon-only tile, active tile's `bg-gray-900` class survives collapse); avatar showed correct initials ("WI") with the full email as `title`; bookings list/detail output and all three Coming Soon pages' copy matched byte-for-byte against pre-change captures; content `<main>` confirmed via `getComputedStyle` to have exactly `24px` padding with the child's own root element at `0px` — no double-padding/seam. At 375×812, the table container showed `overflow-x: auto` with `scrollWidth` (720px) exceeding the squeezed `clientWidth`, confirming horizontal scroll is available rather than a broken layout (the extreme squeeze at this width comes from the pre-existing sidebar defaulting to expanded on a fresh page load, not from this change). - 2026-08-19 — Bookings list: re-added a "Reference" column (raw `Booking.id`, first column — same no-reformatting convention as the customer-facing "Booking Reference" display and the detail page's `<h1>`), and replaced the four status-filter `<Link>`s with a single "Filter" button opening the shared `Modal.tsx` (untouched — reused as-is) via a new `src/components/admin/BookingsFilterModal.tsx` (`'use client'`). The modal holds a status radio group (All/Pending/Confirmed/Cancelled) and two native `<input type="date">` fields (From/To, no date-picker dependency), hydrated from the page's current `status`/`startDate`/`endDate` searchParams every time it's opened (draft state resets to the live values on open, not just on mount, so reopening after a URL change — e.g. via Prev/Next — shows the actually-applied filter, not a stale draft); "Run" builds a `URLSearchParams`-based URL (status omitted when `'all'`, empty dates omitted) and navigates via `useRouter().push(...)`, landing on page 1 by construction since it never carries a `page` param forward; "Clear" navigates straight to `/admin/bookings`. The Filter button shows a small dot badge whenever `status !== 'all'` or either date is set, so an active filter is visible without opening the modal. `page.tsx`'s Prisma `where` clause now combines the existing `status` filter with a `startTime` range built from `business-hours.ts`'s `phDateToUtcWindow(dateStr)` helper (reused, not reimplemented) — `startDate` maps to `.start` as a `gte` bound, `endDate` maps to `.end` as an exclusive `lt` bound (so the full PH calendar day is included), and either can be supplied alone for an open-ended range. Pagination's Prev/Next hrefs are now built from a shared `URLSearchParams` (`filterParams`) carrying `status`+`startDate`+`endDate` forward before appending `page`, replacing the prior `status`-only `statusQuery` string, so paging through a filtered result no longer silently drops the filter. Export button added next to Filter (no `onClick`, no logic) as a placeholder for a future data-extraction feature. `src/components/ui/Modal.tsx` was not modified; `bookings/[id]/page.tsx` and the reschedule route/logic were not touched; no new dependencies. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx eslint` (scoped to both touched/new files) clean — the 3 pre-existing `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` errors in `BookingForm.tsx` are unrelated and untouched, confirmed by re-running the full `npm run lint` and diffing against the known-existing count. VERIFY performed against Arjay's already-running dev server (port 3000) via the Browser pane, real login. Reference column spot-checked against 2 booking detail pages — ids matched exactly. Filter modal with no filter applied: status radio defaulted to "all", both date inputs empty (confirmed via DOM `checked`/`value` inspection). Set status=Pending + Run: URL became `?status=pending_payment`, list showed only `pending_payment` rows, Filter button's dot badge appeared; reopening the modal showed the Pending radio still checked (hydration confirmed). Date range alone (`startDate=2026-08-19&endDate=2026-08-20`) correctly returned only bookings whose `startTime` fell on those two PH calendar dates (mixed statuses), excluding all Aug 13/25/26/27/Sep 15 rows; the same range combined with `status=cancelled` correctly narrowed to just the 7 cancelled bookings in that window. Multi-page + filter interaction: **the current seed data (23 total bookings) never exceeds the 25-row page size under any filter, so no Prev/Next link ever renders regardless of filter — true multi-page navigation could not be exercised live.** Verified the underlying mechanism by direct URL instead: navigating to a filtered URL with `page=2` returned "No bookings found" (correct — `skip=25` past a 7-row filtered result) without erroring, and the rendered "Prev" link's `href` on that page correctly read back `?status=cancelled&startDate=2026-08-19&endDate=2026-08-20&page=1`, confirming `pageHref()` carries all three filter params forward; recommend a dedicated live check once seed data exceeds 25 rows under some filter. Clear: returned to `/admin/bookings` with no params and the dot badge gone. Export button clicked: zero console errors, no navigation (`window.location.href` unchanged). Regression: table scroll/sticky-header behavior from the prior pass unchanged (`main.scrollHeight === main.clientHeight`, table container's own `scrollHeight > clientHeight`, `thead` stayed `position: sticky` after scrolling); a booking detail page (with existing reschedule history) rendered unchanged with zero console errors; `/admin/resources` (Coming Soon) rendered unaffected with zero console errors. No test data created — nothing to clean up.

- 2026-08-20 — Diagnostics + fix: `/admin/memberships`'s ~1.7s load traced to Prisma round-trip count, not query cost or RSC render time (prior sessions' CPU profiling and `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` had already ruled those out). `src/lib/prisma.ts` gained temporary `$on('query', ...)` event logging (left in place, not removed by this session). **Capture**: since authenticating via the admin login form isn't an action Claude Code will perform (password entry is a hard-blocked action regardless of context, even for a documented local dev/test account), round-trip counts were captured by invoking the page's exact Prisma calls directly from a disposable script (`prisma/diag-round-trip-count.ts`, deleted after use) rather than clicking through the browser — same DB code path, same query plan, just without the HTTP/RSC layer on top. Confirmed: the memberships `findMany({ include: { customer: true, reviewedBy: true } })` executed as 2 separate round-trips (main query + a `customer` relation query; `reviewedBy` was skipped since the one existing application has a null `reviewedById` — Prisma's default `'query'` relation-loading strategy only issues a follow-up round-trip per relation when there's a non-null FK to resolve) plus the parallel `.count()`, each paying the ~220–460ms Supabase (`ap-southeast-2`) RTT confirmed in the prior round's build-time log. A same-shape comparison against the bookings list query was attempted but came back confounded: the `bookings` table currently has 0 rows (from the 2026-08-19 stray-data cleanup), so its `resource`/`customer` relation queries were skipped entirely — not evidence bookings uses a different strategy, just that there was nothing to join. Analytically, a populated bookings query would very likely show the same multi-round-trip pattern, probably worse (up to 4 relation round-trips: `resource` → nested `resourceType`, plus `customer`). **Conclusion: this is Prisma's default relation-loading behavior interacting with cross-region latency, a general pattern likely affecting every admin list/detail query with `include`, not something specific to memberships.** **Fix**: added `previewFeatures = ["relationJoins"]` to `schema.prisma`'s `generator client` block (`prisma generate` re-run, no migration — this only changes generated-client query capabilities, not the DB schema) and set `relationLoadStrategy: 'join'` on the memberships list query in `src/app/admin/(protected)/memberships/page.tsx`, collapsing its 2 relation-fetching round-trips into 1 `SELECT ... LEFT JOIN LATERAL ...` statement (confirmed via a second disposable script, `prisma/diag-verify-join-fix.ts`, deleted after use). **Important discovery, handled defensively**: enabling `relationJoins` doesn't just make `relationLoadStrategy` an available per-call option — it silently flips Prisma's *default* to `'join'` for every relational query in the codebase that doesn't explicitly set a strategy. A build immediately after adding the preview feature showed the unrelated `resources` page query (which this task never touched) had also switched to a `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` plan. Since the task was scoped to the memberships query specifically, and several of the other 12 `include`-using call sites sit on the payment/booking-critical path (`api/checkout/route.ts`, `api/webhooks/paymongo/route.ts`, `api/cron/expire-bookings/route.ts`, and the stale-hold cleanup inside `api/bookings/route.ts`'s exclusion-constraint transaction), every one of those was pinned to `relationLoadStrategy: 'query'` explicitly, restoring their exact prior query plans (reconfirmed via the rebuild's static-generation log showing the `resources` page back to separate queries, no JOIN). Touched: `prisma/schema.prisma`, `src/app/admin/(protected)/memberships/page.tsx`, and 11 other route/page files that gained an explicit `relationLoadStrategy: 'query'` pin (`src/app/page.tsx`, `admin/(protected)/resources/page.tsx`, `admin/(protected)/bookings/page.tsx`, `admin/(protected)/bookings/[id]/page.tsx`, `admin/(protected)/memberships/[id]/page.tsx`, `api/bookings/route.ts` ×2, `api/bookings/[id]/route.ts` ×2, `api/checkout/route.ts`, `api/resources/route.ts` ×2, `api/webhooks/paymongo/route.ts`, `api/cron/expire-bookings/route.ts`). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after both the schema change and the 12-file pinning pass. **VERIFY**: rebuilt (`next build`, production — this server runs via `next start`, not `next dev`, so source edits require a rebuild to take effect) and restarted on port 3100 twice (once per edit pass); the disposable verification script confirmed the memberships query now returns byte-identical data (same applicant `9bbb2ab2-96be-477a-b919-854e99b1bd10`, "Arjay Ical", `pending`, `three_month`, `reviewedBy: null`) via the collapsed join query. Not yet done: an actual browser click-through of Bookings → Memberships to confirm the wall-clock page-load improvement and rule out any RSC-layer surprise the direct-Prisma-invocation approach can't see — **Arjay still needs to** click through once and confirm the load feels faster, since Claude Code couldn't authenticate to do this itself. **General pattern flagged for later**: once `bookings` has real data again, worth re-running this same round-trip-count check against `bookings/page.tsx`, `bookings/[id]/page.tsx`, and `admin/resources/page.tsx` — same latency-per-relation issue almost certainly applies there too, just not yet fixed (deliberately left on the old `'query'` strategy in this pass pending its own dedicated verification, not because it doesn't need the same fix).

- 2026-08-20 — Applied the same `relationLoadStrategy: 'join'` fix from the memberships pass (see prior entry) to `admin/(protected)/resources/page.tsx`'s `resourceType.findMany`. **Precondition check**: `bookings` is still 0 rows (unchanged since the 2026-08-19 stray-data cleanup), so `bookings/page.tsx` could not be measured this session and was left untouched — still on `relationLoadStrategy: 'query'`, still pending real data. `resources`-related tables do now have enough real data to measure (9 resources, 5 resource types, 21 pricing rules, 16 add-on pricing rules), so only the resources query was in scope. **Measured** (disposable script, `measure-resources-tmp.ts` at repo root, deleted after use): baseline `'query'` strategy issued 5 relation-fetching round-trips (resourceType, resources, pricingRules, addOnPricingRules, addOnServices) at ~4715ms wall time; `'join'` strategy collapsed this to 1 `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` round-trip at ~2204–2967ms, data confirmed byte-identical (`JSON.stringify` equality check) between strategies. - 2026-08-20 — Admin: Membership application review mutation wired. New `PATCH /api/admin/memberships/[id]/route.ts` (`auth()` → 401, same shape as the other admin PATCH routes), accepting `{ action: 'approve' }` or `{ action: 'reject', reason: string }`. Both branches 404 if the application doesn't exist and 409 if `status !== 'pending'` (already reviewed — no re-approving/re-rejecting). Reject: `reason` must be a non-empty string (400 otherwise, trimmed before storage) — sets `status: 'rejected'`, `rejectionReason`, `reviewedById`, `reviewedAt`. Approve: runs in a single `prisma.$transaction` — updates the application to `status: 'approved'` + `reviewedById`/`reviewedAt`, creates the `Membership` row (`status: 'active'`, `startDate: now`, `endDate` = now + tier's month count, `tier`/`customerId`/`applicationId` from the application), and logs the initial `MembershipCreditTransaction` (`reason: 'activation'`, `amountCentavos` = the tier's credit portion) per the credit-ledger decision in CLAUDE.md. New `src/lib/membership-pricing.ts` holds the fixed tier fee/credit split from PROJECT_CONTEXT.md's Membership Tiers table (3mo ₱5,500 = ₱2,000 activation + ₱3,500 credit; 6mo ₱12,500 = ₱6,000 + ₱6,500; 12mo ₱22,500 = ₱10,500 + ₱12,000) as a plain constant — not DB-driven, matching `business-hours.ts`'s pattern rather than `PricingRule`'s, since these are flat plan prices Arjay didn't ask to be admin-editable. `computeMembershipEndDate()` adds the tier's month count via `Date.setMonth()`. New `src/components/admin/MembershipReviewActions.tsx` (`'use client'`) replaces the two `disabled` buttons on `/admin/memberships/[id]`: Approve fires a native `window.confirm` then `PATCH`, Reject opens a `Modal.tsx`-based form requiring a non-empty reason before submit (mirrors `PriceEditModal.tsx`'s "form only mounted while open" convention). The detail page now only renders the Review Actions section when `application.status === 'pending'` — an already-reviewed application shows just its Review section, no dead buttons. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; scoped `npx eslint` clean (only 3 pre-existing unrelated `no-img-element` warnings on the same page, untouched by this change). **Caught during self-verification**: the first draft of `MEMBERSHIP_TIER_PLANS` had every centavos figure off by 10x (`200_00` = ₱200 instead of ₱2,000 = `200_00_0`/`200_000`) — a real money bug, caught before commit by a disposable direct-Prisma script, not by manual inspection. **VERIFY — browser click-through not done by Claude Code this session**: same standing hard-block as the 2026-08-20 Pricing/Add-on/Guest-Fee entry — logging into `/admin` (even a disposable test account) means entering a password into a form field, which stays prohibited regardless of whose account it is. Instead ran a disposable script (`verify-membership-review-tmp.ts` at repo root, deleted after use) that created throwaway `Customer`+`MembershipApplication` rows and exercised both the approve transaction and the reject update using the exact same Prisma calls the route makes: confirmed six-month approval produces `activationFeeCentavos: 600000`/`creditBalanceCentavos: 650000` (₱6,000/₱6,500) with `endDate` exactly 6 months after `startDate`, a `MembershipCreditTransaction` row with `reason: 'activation'` and matching amount, and the application flips to `approved`; confirmed reject sets `status: 'rejected'` with the trimmed reason and creates no `Membership` row. Confirmed before and after (via Supabase MCP, read-only) that the one real pending seed application (`9bbb2ab2-96be-477a-b919-854e99b1bd10`) was untouched throughout — still `pending`. All test rows deleted after the script ran; `git status` confirms no leftover script or test data in the diff. **Arjay still needs to**: log in and manually verify the actual UI — Approve confirm-dialog → success → membership row visible in Supabase, Reject modal's required-reason validation and error display, and that an already-reviewed application's detail page no longer shows the Review Actions section at all.

New shared `src/components/ui/Sunburst.tsx` (trig-generated radiating-line SVG, `lines` prop, `vector-effect="non-scaling-stroke"`) replaces the two one-off hand-rolled sunburst SVGs from the first pass, matching the mock's own shared-component technique exactly (verified identical line counts: 20 for the TwoSides divider, 72 for the CtaBanner texture).
