- 2026-08-20 — Navbar transparent-on-scroll + TwoSides background fix. **Navbar** (`src/components/layout/Navbar.tsx`): converted from `sticky top-0` (which reserved its own flow space above Hero, so it never actually overlapped the photo) to `fixed inset-x-0 top-0`, so Hero now renders starting at the true top of the viewport with the header floating on top of it — required for the transparent state to actually reveal the Hero photo, not just change the header's own color while sitting in blank space above it. New scroll-tracked `scrolled` state (`window.scrollY > 50`, a plain `scroll` listener registered/cleaned up in `useEffect`, synced once on mount for non-zero initial scroll positions) drives three conditional pieces, all using `transition-colors`/`transition-all duration-200` (matching the duration already used on the nav links themselves) rather than a hard snap: the header's background (`bg-transparent` → `bg-brand-light`), the desktop nav links' text color (`text-accent-light/90` inactive / `text-accent-light` active+hover → the original `text-gray-700`/`text-brand-dark`), and the logo image's filter (`brightness-0 invert` → none). `accent-light` was reused for the nav links because it's the token Hero's own eyebrow line already uses for light text over the same gradient overlay (`Hero.tsx`'s `text-accent-light/90`), not a newly invented color. The logo is a raster PNG (dark brown/gold line art, no separate light-color asset exists in `public/images/brand/`), so "light logo" is approximated via Tailwind's built-in `brightness-0 invert` filter (a filter function, not a hardcoded color) rather than inventing a tinted asset. The "Book a Court"/"Become a Member" buttons, the mobile hamburger button, and the mobile dropdown panel were deliberately left with zero conditional logic — same classes at every scroll position — per the prompt's explicit "same buttons"/"mobile menu... unaffected" scope. Once scrolled past the threshold, every changed class reverts to exactly its original pre-this-prompt value (confirmed via direct className diffing, not just visual). **TwoSides** (`src/components/home/TwoSides.tsx`): section background changed from `bg-background` (the plain page cream, same tone Facilities directly below it uses) to `bg-brand-light` (the distinct "card" cream token) so the two sections now read as visually separate; the small circular sunburst-divider badge (which sits over the section and needs to match its surrounding background to correctly mask the divider line passing behind it) was updated from `bg-background` to `bg-brand-light` in the same edit for the same reason — not a separate content/layout change. No other TwoSides copy/layout/icon/CTA touched. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFY**, done in the Browser pane against `localhost:3000` via direct DOM/className inspection (screenshots and the `computer` click tool both failed with the same long-documented "Browser pane is not displayed, not compositing frames" issue — see the 2026-08-13-onward Next Up entry — so this relied on the same className/JS-driven-click workaround used successfully in prior sessions): at `scrollY: 0`, header confirmed `fixed`/`bg-transparent`, logo confirmed `brightness(0) invert(1)`, active "Home" link confirmed `rgb(245, 230, 211)` (`accent-light` exactly) and inactive links confirmed `accent-light` at 90% opacity; scrolling to `scrollY: 200` (via a dispatched `scroll` event — this environment's programmatic `window.scrollTo` doesn't reliably fire a `scroll` event on its own) confirmed the header/logo/links classNames revert to byte-identical matches of the original pre-prompt Navbar (`bg-brand-light`, no filter classes, `text-brand-dark`/`text-gray-700 hover:text-brand-dark`); scrolling back to `scrollY: 0` confirmed it reverts again to the transparent/light state — both directions work, with a one-JS-call render lag in this environment (state visible on the *next* `javascript_tool` call, not the same one that triggered it) rather than a genuine bug, confirmed by re-querying. All 6 `NAV_LINKS` hrefs and both desktop CTA button hrefs (`/book`, `/membership`) confirmed unchanged. Mobile menu (375px width) toggle confirmed opens/closes correctly via a JS-driven `.click()` (the `computer` tool's click hung on the same pane-display issue) with the hamburger button and dropdown panel classNames confirmed byte-identical to pre-prompt (`text-brand-dark`, `bg-brand-light`, `border-accent-light`) at every scroll position — confirming they're genuinely unaffected, not just visually similar. TwoSides confirmed `bg-brand-light` with Facilities directly below still `bg-background`, and StatsBar/HowItWorks (`bg-brand-dark`)/CtaBanner (gradient) confirmed unchanged. Zero console errors; no horizontal overflow at desktop (1280px, `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 1265`, matching the scrollbar-adjusted viewport) or mobile (375px, `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375`). No test/DB data involved, no cleanup needed.

- 2026-08-20 — TwoSides day-to-night gradient, Navbar scroll-transition smoothing, and a combined gradient-fill/shadow/hover-motion redesign applied to every CTA button across Navbar/Hero/CtaBanner. **TwoSides** (`src/components/home/TwoSides.tsx`): the flat `bg-brand-light` section background (added in the immediately-prior same-day pass) is now a `from-brand-light to-brand-dark` gradient — direction is responsive (`bg-gradient-to-b` on mobile, `md:bg-gradient-to-r` on desktop), not a single fixed left-to-right direction as the originating prompt's literal wording assumed. This was a deliberate correction, not a stylistic choice: the "At the Bar" column's own flex alignment is `items-start` by default and only flips to `md:items-end`/`md:text-right` at the desktop breakpoint, so on mobile its heading/body/link render left-aligned — a fixed horizontal gradient would have placed newly-lightened "At the Bar" text against the *light* end of the gradient on mobile (a real contrast failure), since mobile stacks both columns vertically rather than placing them side by side. Flipping the gradient to vertical on mobile keeps it aligned with actual content order (On the Court on top/light, At the Bar below/dark) without touching any layout/alignment class, preserving the prompt's explicit "no other layout change" constraint; confirmed via the Browser pane at 375px that `background-image` resolves to a top-to-bottom gradient and that the "At the Bar" heading's bounding-box sits below "On the Court"'s. The sunburst-divider badge's background was changed from `bg-brand-light` to `bg-accent-primary` — verified as the correct choice (not the prompt's guessed `brand-mid`) by computing the actual RGB midpoint of the two gradient endpoints (`(164, 145, 137)`) and measuring Euclidean distance against all 6 theme tokens: `accent-primary` (`#C08552`) came out closest (distance ≈63) vs. `brand-mid` (`#8C5A3C`, distance ≈98) — confirmed in-browser that the badge's computed `background-color` is `rgb(192, 133, 82)` (`accent-primary` exactly). The divider's own `Sunburst` texture color was changed from `text-accent-primary/25` to `text-brand-light/25` since the badge background is no longer light — the old combination would have rendered the sunburst lines invisible (same hue as the badge itself). The "&" glyph was evaluated for a color change and deliberately left `text-brand-dark` — WCAG contrast math showed `brand-dark`-on-`accent-primary` (~4.07:1) still beats `brand-light`-on-`accent-primary` (~2.73:1), the only other realistic option, so switching it would have made contrast worse, not better. "At the Bar" column recolored for legibility against its now-dark end: icon and heading to `text-brand-light`, body copy to `text-brand-light/85` (mirroring CtaBanner's own existing heading/subtext token pair exactly), and the "Visit the Café →" link to `text-accent-light/90` hover:`text-accent-light` — this exact dim-to-full-opacity hover pattern was copied from Navbar's own existing transparent-state nav-link treatment (`text-accent-light/90` → `text-accent-light`) rather than inventing a new one, after an initial `accent-light`→`brand-light` hover pairing was rejected for being visually indistinguishable (the two tokens are only 8 hex units apart). "On the Court" (light end) was left completely untouched, and no copy/layout/icon/CTA href changed on either side. **Navbar** (`src/components/layout/Navbar.tsx`): two genuine smoothness bugs fixed, both confirmed via the Browser pane's rule-matching/CSS-inspection workaround (screenshots/computed-style-after-transition still unusable — same non-compositing pane issue documented since 2026-08-13; getComputedStyle reads back the pre-transition value even after a real 500ms wait, so verification relied on confirming the *source* CSS rules and DOM classes are structurally correct rather than sampling mid-transition paint). (1) The scrolled-state background toggle (`bg-transparent` ↔ `bg-brand-light`) was replaced with `bg-brand-light/[0.01]` ↔ `bg-brand-light`, so the transparent end keeps `brand-light`'s actual hue in its color channels instead of the `transparent` keyword's implicit `rgba(0,0,0,0)` — first tried literal `bg-brand-light/0`, but inspecting the compiled stylesheet showed Tailwind's build pipeline optimizes exactly-zero-alpha colors down to `lab(0 0 0 / 0)` (discarding the source hue, since it's visually a no-op for static rendering) — defeating the fix. `/[0.01]` (an explicit non-zero value) survives that optimization; confirmed in the compiled CSS that `.bg-brand-light\/[0.01]` resolves to `lab(96.39 1.98 7.06 / 0.01)`, the correct `brand-light` hue at near-zero opacity, so the fade-in is now a clean same-hue opacity ramp rather than passing through a black-tinted middle. (2) The logo's filter toggle previously swapped between an empty string (`filter: none`) and `brightness-0 invert`, which cannot interpolate smoothly — per the CSS Filter Effects spec, `filter` only animates function-by-function when both states list the same functions, and `none` is a zero-length list. Changed the scrolled-true state from `''` to explicit `brightness-100 invert-0`, so both states always express the same two functions (`brightness(...)  invert(...)`) at different magnitudes, confirmed via the compiled CSS (`--tw-brightness`/`--tw-invert` custom properties feeding the same `filter: var(--tw-blur,) var(--tw-brightness,) ... var(--tw-invert,) ...` composition in both rules) — this is the actual mechanism that enables real interpolation, not just an assumption. Duration/easing (200ms, no explicit easing) was left unchanged — evaluated per the prompt's "only adjust if actually snapping" instruction and no snap was found once the two structural bugs above were fixed; the 50px scroll threshold and every other scroll-driven behavior (which elements change, buttons/mobile-menu being unaffected by scroll state) are untouched. **Buttons** (all 3 files): the same gradient-fill/shadow/lift/arrow treatment now applies to all 4 solid buttons (Navbar desktop "Book a Court", Navbar mobile "Book a Court", Hero "Book Now", CtaBanner "Book Now") and the same fill-in-on-hover treatment applies to all 3 outlined buttons (Navbar desktop/mobile "Become a Member", CtaBanner "Explore Membership") — 7 instances total, confirmed by enumerating every matching `<a>` in the DOM (including opening the mobile menu via a JS-dispatched click, since it's conditionally unmounted when closed) and diffing hrefs/copy against the pre-change originals — all unchanged. Solid buttons: flat `bg-accent-primary` replaced with `bg-gradient-to-r from-accent-primary to-brand-mid` (adjacent same-family tokens, gradient itself does NOT change on hover — CSS can't reliably interpolate between two different gradient stop-color sets, so only shadow/lift/arrow animate), plus `shadow-lg shadow-accent-primary/30` at rest deepening to `shadow-xl shadow-accent-primary/40` on hover (confirmed in compiled CSS these resolve to real accent-primary-tinted shadow colors — `#c085524d`/`#c0855266` — not a default gray), `hover:-translate-y-0.5`, and a new `→` arrow span (`group-hover:translate-x-1`) that wasn't present before. Outlined buttons: since `background-image`/gradient changes aren't transition-animatable, "fill in on hover" is implemented as an absolutely-positioned `-z-10` sibling span (same gradient, `opacity-0` → `group-hover:opacity-100`, `rounded-full`, clipped by the parent's `overflow-hidden`) sitting behind the button's plain-text content via CSS paint order — confirmed present with correct classes on both instances. Navbar's "Become a Member" also flips `text-brand-dark` → `hover:text-brand-light` (its rest-state dark text would be unreadable once the warm gradient fills in); CtaBanner's "Explore Membership" keeps `text-brand-light` unchanged on hover, since it already sits on a dark section and reads fine against the same gradient (matching how the solid buttons' own light text already works against this exact gradient elsewhere). `focus-visible:outline` (previously present on some buttons, absent on others) was added to every instance for consistency, since the prompt's own "read as one cohesive style" goal covers a11y treatment along with color/motion, and this only adds classes — it changes no copy, href, or position. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean before and after. **VERIFY**, Browser pane against `localhost:3000`: TwoSides gradient/badge/text colors confirmed via computed-style + stylesheet-rule inspection at both 1280px and 375px widths (see above); Navbar scroll toggle simulated via `window.scrollTo` + a dispatched `scroll` event (this environment's programmatic scroll doesn't fire a native event on its own — same workaround as the prior Navbar entry), confirming via `element.className`/rule-matching (not `getComputedStyle`, which is stuck on stale pre-transition values in this non-compositing pane) that the correct classes apply at each scroll position; all 7 button instances enumerated with hrefs/copy/classes confirmed; zero console errors; no horizontal overflow at 1280px (`scrollWidth === clientWidth === 1265`) or 375px (`375 === 375`); `git status` confirms only `TwoSides.tsx`/`Navbar.tsx`/`Hero.tsx`/`CtaBanner.tsx` touched — `StatsBar.tsx`/`HowItWorks.tsx`/`Facilities.tsx`/Footer untouched. No test/DB data involved, no cleanup needed. **Still not independently confirmed**: actual smooth visual playback of the Navbar transition and button hover animations — this session's Browser pane has the same non-compositing limitation as every session since 2026-08-13 (screenshots time out, `getComputedStyle` reads stale values through a transition), so this VERIFY relied entirely on confirming the underlying CSS mechanics are structurally sound (matching function lists, non-zero-alpha colors, real hover rules in the compiled stylesheet) rather than watching the animation actually play — worth a quick manual look from Arjay once convenient, though the fixes target root-caused, spec-level interpolation problems rather than guesses.

- 2026-08-20 — Wordmark size bump, Navbar button-height fix, and a restraint pass dialing back the prior gradient/shadow/motion button redesign to a flat, single-tone treatment. **Hero** (`src/components/home/Hero.tsx`): the Parisienne "Winston" wordmark grew one step at each breakpoint (`text-6xl md:text-8xl` → `text-7xl md:text-9xl`, the largest step Tailwind's default scale offers) — eyebrow, subtext, spacing, photo, and button classes are otherwise untouched; confirmed via the Browser pane at both 375px and 1280px that the gap above the wordmark (eyebrow-to-h1) and below it (h1-to-"SIP & SERVE") both stay a clean 16px+ with zero overlap, and computed font-size reads 72px mobile / 128px desktop. **Navbar** (`src/components/layout/Navbar.tsx`): "Book a Court" and "Become a Member" previously rendered at different heights because vertical padding differed (`py-3.5` vs `py-2` desktop); both are now `py-3.5` — applied to the mobile stacked versions too (`py-2.5` → `py-3.5` on both, not just the mismatched pair), so all four instances share one padding value rather than leaving mobile at a second, different-but-matching value. Confirmed in-browser both desktop buttons compute `padding-top`/`padding-bottom: 14px` identically (the remaining 48px-vs-50px bounding-box difference is the outlined button's 1px border on each edge, not a padding mismatch); same confirmed for the mobile stacked pair after opening the menu via a JS-dispatched click (same `computer`-tool-hangs-on-this-pane workaround used in every session since 2026-08-13). **Button styling, all 7 instances** (Navbar desktop/mobile "Book a Court", Hero "Book Now", CtaBanner "Book Now", Navbar desktop/mobile "Become a Member", CtaBanner "Explore Membership"): this deliberately dials back the two-tone-gradient/colored-glow-shadow/hover-lift/arrow-slide treatment from the immediately-prior same-day pass (see previous Completed Log entry above) in favor of a calmer, more restrained look more in keeping with the boutique/elegant brand direction — solid buttons went from `bg-gradient-to-r from-accent-primary to-brand-mid` + `shadow-lg shadow-accent-primary/30` (deepening to `/40` on hover) + `hover:-translate-y-0.5` + a `group-hover:translate-x-1` sliding arrow to a flat `bg-accent-primary` with no shadow at all, `hover:bg-brand-mid` as the sole hover feedback, and a static (non-animated) arrow glyph — the hover lift was dropped too once color-deepening became the primary feedback signal, since keeping it alongside a flat fill read busier, not calmer. Outlined buttons ("Become a Member" ×2, "Explore Membership") dropped the absolutely-positioned gradient-sweep span entirely (`opacity-0` → `group-hover:opacity-100` overlay) in favor of a direct `hover:border-accent-primary hover:bg-accent-primary` background-color transition — simpler markup (no extra span, no `relative isolate overflow-hidden`/`group` scaffolding) and a cleaner transition, since `background-color` animates natively without needing a painted-behind sibling. `focus-visible:outline` was also added to the two mobile instances that had been missing it (mobile "Book a Court" and mobile "Become a Member"), so all 7 now carry it consistently. No copy, href, or position changed on any instance — confirmed via full-DOM enumeration of every `a.rounded-full` before/after. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFY**, Browser pane against `localhost:3000` (reused an already-running dev server on port 3000 rather than starting a second one, which Next.js refused since one was already up): confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that all three solid-button instances resolve to flat `rgb(192, 133, 82)` (`accent-primary`) with `background-image: none` and `box-shadow: none`; confirmed all three outlined instances have `background-color: rgba(0,0,0,0)` at rest with the `hover:bg-accent-primary hover:border-accent-primary` rule present in their className; confirmed desktop and mobile "Book a Court"/"Become a Member" both compute `14px`/`14px` padding; confirmed the wordmark size bump and its spacing at 375px/1280px (above); zero console errors; no horizontal overflow at 1280px (`1265 === 1265`) or 375px (`375 === 375`); `git status` confirms only `Hero.tsx`/`Navbar.tsx`/`CtaBanner.tsx` touched — `TwoSides.tsx`/`StatsBar.tsx`/`HowItWorks.tsx`/`Facilities.tsx`/Footer untouched. Actual hover-state animation playback (the `background-color` transition itself, not just the rule's presence) is unverified for the same reason as every prior session — the Browser pane's non-compositing limitation (documented since 2026-08-13) prevents sampling mid-transition paint — but this pass structurally removed the two mechanisms that previously needed workarounds for that reason (the gradient shadow-color deepening and the opacity-sweep span), so there's less left to verify visually than before. No test/DB data involved, no cleanup needed.

- 2026-08-20 — Button radius/size/legibility pass, closing out Home's button treatment. **Radius** (all 7 instances — Navbar desktop/mobile "Book a Court"/"Become a Member", Hero "Book Now", CtaBanner "Book Now"/"Explore Membership"): `rounded-full` → `rounded-lg` — same fill/border/hover behavior, corner radius only; confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that every instance now computes `border-radius: 8px`. **Navbar resize** (`src/components/layout/Navbar.tsx`): "Book a Court" and "Become a Member" previously matched Hero's own standalone-CTA sizing (`py-3.5`, `px-9`/`px-5`), which read oversized sitting inline next to the text nav links — both shrunk to `py-2.5` (desktop and mobile stacked versions alike, so all four instances stay in sync) with horizontal padding trimmed too (`px-9`→`px-6` on "Book a Court"; "Become a Member" kept `px-5`, its shorter label not needing further trimming); vertical padding kept equal between the two so the cross-button height-parity fix from two passes ago isn't undone — confirmed both desktop buttons compute identical `10px`/`10px` padding-top/bottom (40px/42px bounding height, the 2px gap being "Become a Member"'s 1px border on each edge, same relationship as before this pass). Hero's "Book Now" and both CtaBanner buttons were deliberately left at their existing `px-9 py-3.5` size — this resize is Navbar-specific. **"Become a Member" legibility** (`src/components/layout/Navbar.tsx`): previously hardcoded `text-brand-dark`/`border-brand-mid` at every scroll position, illegible against the Hero photo when the header is transparent at the top of the page — now conditional on the existing `scrolled` state, extending the same pattern the nav links already use: `border-accent-light/60 text-accent-light` when unscrolled (matching the nav links' own `text-accent-light`/`/90` treatment for the transparent header), reverting to `border-brand-mid text-brand-dark` once past the 50px scroll threshold, on the same `transition-colors duration-300` already in place. The existing `hover:border-accent-primary hover:bg-accent-primary hover:text-brand-light` state was left untouched — it already reads correctly in both scroll states, since it overrides border/text/background outright regardless of the base conditional. The mobile stacked version was deliberately left non-conditional: confirmed the mobile dropdown panel (`bg-brand-light`, opened via a JS-dispatched click on `button[aria-label="Toggle menu"]`, same workaround as every session since the Browser pane stopped compositing) is unconditionally solid at every scroll position, so `text-brand-dark`/`border-brand-mid` is already legible there — adding the same conditional would have been dead code. No copy, href, or position changed on any of the 7 instances — confirmed via full-DOM enumeration before/after. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFY**, Browser pane against `localhost:3000`: confirmed all 7 instances compute `border-radius: 8px`; confirmed Navbar's two desktop buttons at 40px/42px height (down from 48px/50px) with matching `10px` vertical padding; confirmed Hero's "Book Now" (48px) and both CtaBanner buttons (50px) unchanged from before this pass; confirmed via `className` inspection (not `getComputedStyle`, which reads stale pre-transition values in this non-compositing pane — same documented workaround as every session since 2026-08-13) that "Become a Member" correctly switches between `border-accent-light/60 text-accent-light` at `scrollY: 0` and `border-brand-mid text-brand-dark` at `scrollY: 200`, and reverts cleanly back to the unscrolled classes when scrolled back to `0`; confirmed the mobile panel's `background-color` is `rgb(253, 243, 231)` (`brand-light`, solid) and its "Become a Member" instance is unconditionally `border-brand-mid text-brand-dark` at `rounded-lg`/42px height; zero console errors; no horizontal overflow at 1280px (`1265 === 1265`) or 375px (`375 === 375`); `git status` confirms only `Navbar.tsx`/`Hero.tsx`/`CtaBanner.tsx` touched — `TwoSides.tsx`/`StatsBar.tsx`/`HowItWorks.tsx`/`Facilities.tsx`/Footer untouched. This closes out Home's button treatment — no further radius/size/color work flagged for these 7 instances. No test/DB data involved, no cleanup needed.

- 2026-08-20 — Café & Bar page built: `/cafe-bar` (`src/app/cafe-bar/page.tsx`) plus 5 new components under `src/components/cafe-bar/` (`CafeBarHero`, `MenuHighlights`, `SpeakeasyFeature`, `Gallery`, `HoursLocation`). **Composition**: confirmed Home (`src/app/page.tsx`) composes `Navbar`/`Footer` itself rather than a shared root layout (`src/app/layout.tsx` is bare — just fonts/body), so `cafe-bar/page.tsx` mirrors that exact pattern (`<Navbar /><CafeBarHero />...<Footer />`) rather than assuming a global wrapper; `/book` and `/membership` were checked too and turned out to render with *no* Navbar/Footer at all (a pre-existing gap on those two pages, left untouched — out of scope here) — Home was the correct pattern to mirror, per the prompt's own instruction. **Navbar legibility**: confirmed Navbar's transparent-at-top/solid-on-scroll toggle is purely `window.scrollY`-based and global (not route-scoped), so `CafeBarHero` uses a `bg-brand-dark` section from the very top — same fix already applied to Home's Hero — keeping the unscrolled header's light (`text-accent-light`) nav text legible without waiting for the scroll threshold. **Copy/scope guardrails**: "members-only" text appears only in `SpeakeasyFeature.tsx` (a badge + body copy) — Hero/MenuHighlights/Gallery/HoursLocation all read as open-to-all; hours render as explicit placeholders ("Hours: To be announced" in `HoursLocation`, "Evening hours — details coming soon." in `SpeakeasyFeature`), no "6AM"/"10PM" string anywhere in the new files (Footer's existing "Open Daily: 6:00 AM – 10:00 PM" copy was left untouched, per the prompt's explicit deferral of that conflict). `HoursLocation` reuses Footer's address/phone/email `CONTACT_ITEMS` entries inline (same values, same duplication-over-shared-constant pattern Footer itself already uses) but deliberately drops Footer's hours entry, since that one does say 6AM–10PM. `Icons.tsx` gained one additive `CocktailIcon` (hand-authored SVG, same `viewBox="0 0 24 24"`/`stroke="currentColor"`/`strokeWidth="1.5"` convention as the existing icons) for the Speakeasy section — no existing cocktail/moon icon was found, no other icon touched. `Gallery.tsx` reuses `/images/placeholder.jpg` with varied `objectPosition` and descriptive `alt` text (café interior, bar/lounge, speakeasy entrance, outdoor patio), same pattern as `Facilities.tsx`. No Prisma import anywhere in the new files — this is a fully static content page. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFY**: grepped all new files for `dark:`/`border-black`/`bg-foreground`/`text-background`/`green` (zero matches), `6AM`/`10PM`/`10 PM`/`6:00 AM`/`10:00 PM` (zero matches), and `members-only` (exactly one file, `SpeakeasyFeature.tsx`) — all as expected. Browser pane confirmed `/cafe-bar` renders full content (no 404, `get_page_text` dump matches all intended copy) and `window.location.pathname === '/cafe-bar'` with the "Cafe & Bar" nav link carrying the active-state class (`text-accent-light` + underline bar at `scrollY: 0`, matching `CafeBarHero`'s dark background). Regression: Home (`/`) and `/book` both reloaded and confirmed rendering unchanged via `get_page_text`/zero console errors; Footer's and TwoSides' "Cafe & Bar"/"Visit the Café →" links now resolve to real content instead of 404ing. **Scroll-state legibility caveat**: confirming the *solid-header* scrolled state's colors hit the same non-compositing Browser-pane limitation documented since 2026-08-13, with a new specific cause diagnosed this session — `document.visibilityState` reports `"hidden"` for this pane, so the tab is genuinely backgrounded and Chrome throttles paint/transitions, making `getComputedStyle` return stale/interpolated values even though the underlying `className` (the real signal for which Tailwind rule applies) updates correctly and instantly. Verified via `className` inspection instead (not `getComputedStyle`), same workaround prior sessions used, and cross-checked the identical scrolled-state class transition on Home to confirm it's a pre-existing Navbar characteristic, not something this page's markup introduced. No test/DB data involved, no cleanup needed.

- 2026-08-20 — Café & Bar page gained a client-side Café/Bar mode switch, replacing the prior single static composition. New `src/components/cafe-bar/CafeBarPageClient.tsx` (`'use client'`) owns `const [mode, setMode] = useState<'cafe' | 'bar'>('cafe')` (local component state only — no URL param, no localStorage) and composes `CafeBarHero` (now takes `mode`/`onModeChange` props) followed by `MenuHighlights` (mode === 'cafe') or `SpeakeasyFeature` (mode === 'bar'), then `Gallery` (now takes `mode`); `src/app/cafe-bar/page.tsx` renders `<CafeBarPageClient />` followed by the unchanged `HoursLocation`, which stays outside the mode boundary. New `src/components/cafe-bar/ModeSwitch.tsx` — a two-segment pill of plain `<button>`s with `aria-pressed` (the simpler fallback pattern, since the one existing tab-like control in the codebase, `ResourcesTabs.tsx`, already leans on plain buttons + an aria attribute rather than a full `role="radiogroup"` tablist) — rendered inside `CafeBarHero` under the headline. **Hero copy**: Café keeps the existing eyebrow/headline/body minus the "Cocktails" reference (eyebrow trimmed to "Craft Coffee & Conversation"); Bar gets a new eyebrow ("Members-Only Speakeasy"), headline ("Winston After Dark"), and one atmosphere-only paragraph (no menu-item language) matching `TwoSides.tsx`'s existing "craft coffee by day, a members-only speakeasy by night" tone. **Café menu** (`MenuHighlights.tsx` reworked): 3 real categories (Espresso, Specialty, Bites) with 2–4 hardcoded items each (`{ name, priceCentavos, description, image: '/images/placeholder.jpg' }`), prices formatted via the existing `formatCentavos` from `src/lib/format.ts` (no new formatter written) — Café Americano ₱125/Flat White ₱150 (Espresso), Winston Barrel Brew ₱170/Matcha Latte ₱180 (Specialty), Winston Club ₱310/Tropical Bowl ₱250/Avocado Toast ₱270/Trail Mix Plate ₱200 (Bites) — each item card carries a small `/images/placeholder.jpg` thumbnail (`Facilities.tsx`'s `object-cover` pattern). **Bar mode** (`SpeakeasyFeature.tsx` extended in place, not duplicated): kept its existing dark treatment/"Members-Only" badge/TBD-hours line, added 3 tier highlight cards with figures pulled verbatim from PROJECT_CONTEXT.md's Membership Tiers section (3-month ₱5,500 = ₱2,000 activation + ₱3,500 credit; 6-month ₱12,500 = ₱6,000 + ₱6,500; 12-month ₱22,500 = ₱10,500 + ₱12,000), a Speakeasy-specific perks line (exclusive access, F&B credit usable at the bar), and kept the existing "Explore Membership" → `/membership` CTA (already on the site's flat single-tone `rounded-lg`/`bg-accent-primary`/`hover:bg-brand-mid` button treatment, no change needed there). All of this stayed inside the original single flex-centered `Reveal` wrapper rather than being split into separate top-level `Reveal`s, since the outer `text-center` div relies on `flex flex-col items-center` (not `text-align`) to center its block-level children — splitting would have left the new tier-cards/perks/CTA content unintentionally left-aligned. **Gallery** (`Gallery.tsx` reworked): now takes a `mode` prop, swaps both the tile set and `alt`/caption text — Café: "Café interior seating"/"Coffee bar counter"/"Daytime lounge view"; Bar: "Speakeasy entrance"/"Bar and lounge seating"/"Evening ambiance" (3 tiles per mode, down from the prior 4 static ones) — and gained a gradient-overlay tint (`Facilities.tsx`'s `bg-gradient-to-t from-brand-dark/X via-brand-dark/10 to-transparent` technique) at a stronger opacity on Bar tiles (`/75` vs. `/40`) to reinforce the night framing. **Find Us** (`HoursLocation.tsx`): the Location column's itemized address/phone/email list (which duplicated Footer verbatim) was replaced with a single `/images/placeholder.jpg` styled as a map embed (`aspect-[16/9]`, rounded corners, `shadow-card` matching the site's card treatment) captioned "Map — embed coming soon.", plus one neighborhood label above it ("East Fairview Park Subdivision") — the Hours column and everything else on the page is untouched. `Icons.tsx` was not touched — the existing `CocktailIcon` (added in the prior Café & Bar prompt) already covers Bar mode's icon need. No new npm dependencies, no `tailwind.config.js`, no Prisma import in any touched file. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **VERIFY**: grepped all touched files for `dark:`/`border-black`/`bg-foreground`/`text-background`/`green`/`6AM`/`6:00 AM`/`10PM`/`10:00 PM` — zero matches. Browser pane confirmed (via the dev server already running on port 3000 — a second `next dev` refused to start alongside it, same as noted in an earlier session): default load is Café mode (real menu items, café hero copy, no membership CTA); toggling to Bar mode (via a JS-dispatched `.click()` on the button — the pane's synthetic `computer` mouse click silently no-ops on this control, the same pre-existing non-compositing/input limitation documented since 2026-08-13, confirmed by `aria-pressed` staying unchanged after a `computer`-tool click at the exact same coordinate) updates Hero copy, all 3 gallery `alt` attributes, and the menu region (tier cards + CTA, no leftover café items) together; toggling back to Café fully reverts with no stale Bar content; tier figures confirmed byte-exact against PROJECT_CONTEXT.md; CTA href confirmed `/membership`; Hours & Location confirmed showing the TBD hours placeholder plus the new map placeholder/neighborhood label, no duplicated contact info. Home (`/`) and `/book` reloaded and confirmed unaffected (zero console errors); Navbar's "Cafe & Bar" active-tab class confirmed present regardless of mode, since the mode switch is purely local component state with no route change. **Verification gap**: real keyboard-driven (Enter/Space) activation of the switch was not directly exercised through the pane's `key` action — it also had no visible effect, consistent with the same input-delivery limitation as the mouse click — so this relied on confirming the switch uses plain semantic `<button>` elements with no keydown interception (keyboard activation of a native `<button>` is guaranteed by the browser, not by app code) rather than watching it happen; worth a quick manual Tab+Enter check from Arjay once convenient. No test/DB data involved, no cleanup needed. **Known follow-ups, unchanged from the prior Café & Bar entry**: real menu photos, real map embed, and now also real membership-CTA copy polish are still placeholder/deferred — not blockers.

- 2026-08-20 — News page built (`/news`), closing out the "News — Not started" gap and completing all 6 marketing pages. This build deliberately reversed the usual schema-first approach: Arjay asked for the frontend content shape to be nailed down first, with the database question (reuse the existing `Bulletin` model vs. a new one) left as an explicit future decision — no `prisma/schema.prisma` edit, no migration, no API route, fully static/hardcoded content, per the originating prompt's scope. `/news` (`src/app/news/page.tsx`) composes `Navbar` → `NewsHero` → `NewsGrid` → `Footer`, the same per-page pattern as `about/page.tsx`/`cafe-bar/page.tsx` (confirmed via direct read). Three new components under `src/components/news/`: `NewsHero.tsx` (mirrors `AboutHero.tsx`'s `bg-brand-dark pt-40 pb-20 md:pt-48 md:pb-28` hero exactly, no CTA button — eyebrow "What's Happening", headline "News & Announcements"); `NewsGrid.tsx` (defines the local `NewsCategory`/`NewsItem` types and the hardcoded `NEWS_ITEMS` array inside the component holding it, following `MenuHighlights.tsx`'s convention rather than a separate data file — `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-3 gap-8`, each card wrapped in the existing `Reveal` scroll-animation component); `NewsCard.tsx` (image tile using `Facilities.tsx`'s `aspect-[4/3]`/`object-cover`/gradient-scrim convention, a category badge reusing `TierCards.tsx`'s "Most Popular" solid-pill treatment (`bg-accent-primary`/`text-brand-light`/`rounded-full`) positioned over the image rather than inventing a new badge style, title/excerpt (`line-clamp-3`, a built-in Tailwind v4 core utility — no plugin needed, confirmed no other codebase usage to conflict with) /date with the existing `CalendarIcon` from `Icons.tsx`, and a social row rendered only when `socialUrl` is present). **Social icons**: `SocialIcons.tsx` always renders both Facebook and Instagram in a row with no way to select a single platform, so rather than hand-authoring new icon paths or modifying that shared component (out of scope — see below), `NewsCard.tsx` copies its exact Facebook/Instagram `path` `d` values verbatim into a local `SOCIAL_ICON_PATHS` const, with a code comment documenting the reuse and why. All 4 hardcoded items match the originating prompt exactly (Renovation/Tournament/Closure/Community, with the Closure item deliberately omitting `socialPlatform`/`socialUrl` to prove the card's optional-field handling) — a `// SAMPLE CONTENT` comment block above the array flags every item as placeholder, and every `socialUrl` is literally `'#'`, never a guessed real link. Navbar's `NAV_LINKS` and Footer's `QUICK_LINKS` already both had a "News" → `/news` entry (from the original 2026-08-13 build, confirmed via direct read) — neither file was touched. No new npm dependencies, no `tailwind.config.js`, no Prisma import. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; grepped `src/app/news/` and `src/components/news/` for `dark:`/`border-black`/`bg-foreground`/`text-background`/`green` — zero matches. **VERIFY** (Browser pane, `get_page_text`/`javascript_exec` fallback per this file's standing non-compositing-frames note — `computer` screenshot confirmed still unavailable in this session): `/news` renders Navbar → dark hero → exactly 4 cards (titles/categories/dates all present) → Footer, no 404; confirmed via DOM query that exactly 3 of 4 cards render a "View on Instagram"/"View on Facebook" link and the Closure card renders none; confirmed all 3 rendered social `href` attributes are exactly `"#"`; confirmed via `className` inspection that Navbar's "News" link carries the active-tab class (`text-accent-light` + `after:absolute` underline) on desktop and (`text-brand-dark` vs. `text-gray-700` siblings) in the mobile menu, both only on `/news`. Mobile (375×812): `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375` (no overflow), grid computed to 1 column. Desktop (1280px): no overflow, grid's `getComputedStyle().gridTemplateColumns` confirmed 3 equal columns. Regression: reloaded `/`, `/book`, `/cafe-bar`, `/membership`, `/about` — all render with zero console errors. `git status` confirmed only `src/app/news/` and `src/components/news/` (both new/untracked) were touched; `git diff --stat prisma/schema.prisma` confirmed zero diff. No test/DB data created (fully static page, no Prisma import) — no cleanup step needed.
  **Current `Bulletin` model, for direct comparison against the new `NewsItem` shape**: `id` (cuid), `title` (String), `body` (String), `isPublished` (Boolean, default false), `publishedAt` (DateTime?), `createdAt`/`updatedAt`. This is a flat title/body/publish-state shape with no category, image, excerpt/body split, or social-link fields at all — the new frontend-driven `NewsItem` shape (`category`, `image`, `excerpt` distinct from a fuller body, `date` display string, optional `socialPlatform`/`socialUrl`) is meaningfully richer than what `Bulletin` currently models. Whether the eventual DB design extends `Bulletin` with these fields or introduces a separate model is the explicit open decision this build was built to inform, not resolve.

- **Flagged for Arjay's confirmation, not buried above**: the News page build deliberately reversed this project's usual schema-first order — frontend content shape (`NewsItem`) was designed first, hardcoded, and shipped with the DB question (extend `Bulletin` vs. new model) left open, rather than designing the schema first and building the UI against it. This may be a broader workflow change Arjay wants going forward (not just a one-off for News) — if so, CLAUDE.md's Development Workflow section should be updated to reflect a "frontend-shape-first" option alongside the existing schema-first default. Left unresolved here since only Arjay can confirm whether this is a one-time exception or a standing preference.

- 2026-08-21 — Home's stat-banner latency fixed by dropping its DB dependency entirely, not by caching it. An earlier same-day attempt to fix this via `unstable_cache`/`revalidateTag` was drafted but discarded before being run or committed (`git status` confirmed clean, no trace) — superseded by a simpler decision: `src/app/page.tsx`'s `Home()` no longer calls `prisma.resourceType.findMany(...)` at all (no longer `async`, no `prisma` import) and `src/components/home/StatsBar.tsx` now renders its "Sports"/"Courts & Simulators" figures from two hardcoded module-level constants (`TOTAL_RESOURCES = 9`, `SPORT_COUNT = 3`) instead of props — `StatsBar` no longer takes `totalResources`/`sportCount` props at all. Opens Daily/Last Booking (still derived from `src/lib/business-hours.ts`) were left untouched. CLAUDE.md's Architecture Decisions gained a new bullet documenting this as deliberate (fixed single-venue inventory, rare/manual admin-driven changes, time-sensitive facility news already covered by `/book`'s announcement gate) and pointing at both places to update by hand if the inventory ever changes. Separately, `/book`'s dev-mode double-fetch of `GET /api/resources` (React Strict Mode's mount→cleanup→remount firing two real requests, since the prior `cancelled` boolean guarded state updates but never actually cancelled the in-flight request) was fixed in `src/app/book/page.tsx` by replacing the boolean with a real `AbortController` — `controller.abort()` in the effect cleanup, `{ signal: controller.signal }` passed to `fetch`, and the `catch`/`finally` blocks now check for `AbortError`/`controller.signal.aborted` so the discarded first run's rejection never surfaces an error or a premature `loading` flip. No changes to `GET /api/resources` itself, the exclusion constraint, RLS, or any other marketing page. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. No test data or temporary scripts involved.

- 2026-08-21 — Home mobile-responsiveness audit performed (audit-only, no fixes) across 320/375/414/639px widths via Playwright MCP; findings written to `docs/audits/2026-08-21-home-mobile-audit.md`. No component/page/CSS files touched. Confirmed no custom breakpoints in `globals.css`'s `@theme` block (Tailwind's stock 640/768/1024 `sm`/`md`/`lg` apply throughout). No horizontal overflow found at any width. One blocking issue found: TwoSides' "Book a Court →" link (`text-accent-primary`) is nearly unreadable (~1.07:1 contrast, confirmed by contrast-ratio math and screenshot) at all four mobile widths, because the section's mobile-only vertical gradient (`bg-gradient-to-b`, `TwoSides.tsx`) places the link's own color almost exactly on top of the background color at that scroll position — a gap in the same day's earlier day-to-night-gradient work (see the second 2026-08-20 Completed Log entry), which only accounted for text-color contrast on the "At the Bar" (dark-end) column, not "On the Court"'s own link against the vertical gradient's midpoint. Four polish-level findings also logged (mobile-menu header/panel opacity mismatch when opened unscrolled, hamburger tap target at 40×40px, short text-link tap targets in TwoSides/Footer, StatsBar's "Courts & Simulators" label wrapping to 2 lines against 1-line siblings) — full detail and per-section/per-width breakdown in the audit doc. `git status` confirmed only the audit doc and this file changed.

All 7 buttons (Add + 3×Edit + 3×Delete) confirmed `disabled` in the DOM; JS-dispatched `.click()` on all 7 produced zero new network requests and zero new console errors. Regression: `/admin`, `/admin/bookings`, `/admin/resources`, `/admin/memberships` all reloaded with zero console errors; `/news` and `/book` untouched by this prompt, not re-verified beyond that. Mobile (375px): no horizontal overflow (`scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375`) and no element overlap, but the thumbnail visually shrank to ~12×64px — traced this to the *pre-existing, already-documented* sidebar-squeeze issue (2026-08-19 Completed Log entry: sidebar defaults expanded, leaving only ~95px for the content card at 375px) rather than anything in this page's own layout; `main`'s own width was independently confirmed at 95.2px, matching that entry's own measurement almost exactly. - 2026-08-21 — `Bulletin` model extended to a unified schema, matching News/AnnouncementGate's already-established field shape (schema + infrastructure only, no CRUD, no component changes). `prisma/schema.prisma`: added `excerpt` (String, required), `category` (new `BulletinCategory` enum — `Renovation`/`Closure`/`Tournament`/`Community`, exact casing matched to `NewsGrid.tsx`'s `NewsCategory` literal union so no display-string mapping layer is needed), `imageUrl` (String, required, `@map("image_url")`), `socialPlatform`/`socialUrl` (both `String?`, `@map`'d). `publishedAt` deliberately left `DateTime?` — not made required at the schema level, per explicit instruction (a bulletin can exist as an unpublished draft with no publish date; "every published row has a real date" is an application-logic rule for a future prompt, not a NOT NULL constraint). The normal `prisma migrate dev` flow hit the same shadow-DB failure CLAUDE.md already documents (an earlier migration inserts into Supabase-managed `storage.buckets`, which doesn't exist on a fresh shadow DB — this blocks shadow-DB replay for *any* new migration once that migration is in history, not just ones that themselves touch `storage.buckets`) — so, per the documented workaround, the column/enum migration was hand-written (`prisma/migrations/20260821010000_extend_bulletin_model/migration.sql`) and applied via `prisma migrate deploy`, which skips the shadow database. Second part: a new public `bulletin-images` Supabase Storage bucket was provisioned, mirroring the private `membership-applications` bucket's migration structure (`prisma/manual-sql/bulletin-images-bucket.sql`, deliberately kept isolated from the `prisma migrate dev`-generated folder, same convention as `booking-exclusion-constraint.sql`) — `public = true` (the one deliberate difference from the reference bucket), one new RLS policy (`public_read_bulletin_images`, SELECT, `anon`+`authenticated`, scoped to `bucket_id = 'bulletin-images'`) for public read access; no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policy was added, mirroring the reference bucket's pattern of relying on default-deny RLS for non-service roles rather than an explicit write-restriction policy. Applied via its own hand-written migration folder + `prisma migrate deploy`, same shadow-DB-bypass reasoning as above. No new env var needed — `src/lib/supabase-storage.ts`'s existing `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` pair already parameterizes the bucket name per call. VERIFY: a disposable Prisma script (`information_schema.columns` + `pg_enum` query, deleted after use) confirmed all 5 new `bulletins` columns with correct types/nullability and `BulletinCategory` with exactly the 4 expected values, no more/fewer; `Bulletin` row count confirmed still 0 (no CRUD exists yet to have created any). - 2026-08-21 — Bulletin admin CRUD fully wired, replacing the read-only frame from the prior pass with real Create/Edit/Delete and image upload, closing out the admin side of the Bulletin feature. `src/lib/supabase-storage.ts` gained one thin addition, `getPublicUrl(bucket, path)`, constructing Supabase's public-object URL pattern — `uploadToStorage`/`deleteFromStorage` reused verbatim, unchanged. Two new API routes follow the established admin-route shape (`auth()` → 401, `findUnique` existence check → 404 on the `[id]` route, parse → 400, mutate → 200/201), each self-contained with its own local validators rather than a shared helper file, matching `resources/route.ts` vs. `resources/[id]/route.ts`'s existing convention: `POST /api/admin/bulletin` validates `title`/`excerpt`/`body` (non-empty), `category` (exactly one of the 4 enum values), `isPublished` (the literal string `'true'`/`'false'`, set explicitly by the client rather than relying on native-checkbox on/off semantics), `socialPlatform`/`socialUrl` (must be provided together, platform must be `instagram`/`facebook`), and a required `image` file (JPEG/PNG, ≤5MB — same limits as the membership-application upload, not loosened) — uploads the image first (path `{uuid}/image.{ext}`, a client-generated id also used as the `Bulletin.id` so the path is known before insert, same technique `membership-applications/route.ts` already uses), then creates the row with `publishedAt` server-computed (`new Date()` if publishing, `null` otherwise — never accepted from the client). `PATCH /api/admin/bulletin/[id]` reuses the same field validation, treats `image` as optional (uploads a new `{id}/image-{timestamp}.{ext}` object and swaps `imageUrl` only if a file was actually provided, otherwise leaves the existing `imageUrl` untouched), and best-effort-deletes the old Storage object after a successful swap (log-and-continue, never blocking the response — `deleteFromStorage` already does this internally). `DELETE /api/admin/bulletin/[id]` deletes the row then best-effort-deletes its Storage image the same way. **`publishedAt` logic, the trickiest part of this slice**: the correct trigger for auto-setting `publishedAt` to `new Date()` is "this bulletin has never been published before" (`existing.publishedAt === null`), not "was just unpublished" (`existing.isPublished === false`) — an initial implementation used the latter and was caught live during VERIFY (see below): unpublishing then republishing the same bulletin incorrectly bumped `publishedAt` to a new timestamp instead of preserving the original one, exactly the regression the spec called out by name. Fixed by keying the check on `existing.publishedAt` instead of `existing.isPublished`, then re-verified the unpublish→republish cycle to confirm the date now genuinely holds. `src/components/admin/BulletinFormModal.tsx` (new) follows `ResourceFormModal.tsx`/`PriceEditModal.tsx`'s conventions exactly (`Modal.tsx` wrapper, `mode: 'add' | 'edit'`, form mounted only while open, no `useEffect` reset) — Title/Excerpt/Body/Category/Image/Social Platform/Social URL/Published fields, a local `URL.createObjectURL` preview for a newly chosen file (falling back to the existing `imageUrl` in edit mode), client-side pre-submit checks mirroring the server's rules for immediate feedback (server 400 remains the source of truth, not skipped), and a `FormData` submit via `fetch` with no manually-set `Content-Type` (left to the browser for correct multipart boundaries) — on failure the server's `error` string is shown inline and every entered field is preserved, never reset. `src/components/admin/BulletinList.tsx` (new, `'use client'`) is the extracted interactive list — same page.tsx-stays-server / interactive-bits-extracted-to-a-client-component split `resources/page.tsx` → `ResourcesTabs.tsx` already established — receiving the server-fetched `bulletins` array as a prop, owning the "+ Add Bulletin" button and per-row Edit/Delete (delete uses the same `window.confirm(...)` + `DELETE` + `router.refresh()` pattern as Resource delete), with one shared `BulletinFormModal` instance toggled via `addModalOpen`/`editingBulletin` state (same sharing pattern `ResourceTypeCard` uses). `src/app/admin/(protected)/bulletin/page.tsx` is now a thin server component (just the `prisma.bulletin.findMany` call, the page heading, and an updated italic helper note confirming Add/Edit/Delete are live) rendering `<BulletinList bulletins={bulletins} />` — no `'use client'` needed there anymore. No schema changes, no other admin route/page touched. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean both before and after the `publishedAt` fix; scoped `npx eslint` on every new/touched file shows only the same pre-existing `@next/next/no-img-element` warning tolerated elsewhere in this admin panel (not suppressed). **VERIFY**, all live against the dev server via the Browser pane (this environment's file-picker gap — same one documented in the 2026-08-20 Membership-application entry — worked around by base64-encoding two disposable local test PNGs and injecting them into the file inputs via a script-driven `DataTransfer`/`File` construction, same technique as before), logged in as `winstonsipandserve@gmail.com`: created a fully-filled published bulletin (Instagram social link included) — got 201, `publishedAt` auto-set to ~now, the image's real public Supabase URL rendered and loaded (`naturalWidth` confirmed non-zero) in the list; created a second bulletin with `isPublished: false` — confirmed `publishedAt: null` and the gray Draft pill; edited the draft to published — `publishedAt` became non-null; edited the same row again touching only the excerpt — `publishedAt` unchanged, `updatedAt` bumped, confirming the "stays true" branch doesn't overwrite; unpublished it — `publishedAt` preserved, not cleared; republished it — **this is where the `existing.isPublished`-vs-`existing.publishedAt` bug above was caught and fixed**, then the same unpublish→republish cycle was re-run and `publishedAt` was confirmed genuinely unchanged this second time. Replaced the first bulletin's image via edit — confirmed via Supabase MCP that `imageUrl` pointed at the new object and the old one was gone from `storage.objects` (both this replacement and the accidental one below cleaned up correctly, confirming the replace-and-delete-old-image path works in both cases). Validation, all via direct `fetch` bypassing the client (server-side validation confirmed independent of client trust): missing title → 400 `"Title is required"`; `socialPlatform` set with no `socialUrl` → 400 `"socialPlatform and socialUrl must be provided together"`; an invalid category string sent directly against the API → 400, confirming the client `<select>`'s options aren't the only enforcement. - 2026-08-21 — `/account` Recent Bookings horizontal-scrollbar fix + QR "View Full Screen" modal + Navbar button reorder, **still hardcoded-sample-data only, no backend**. Three independent fixes, no pagination/row-content/schema/dependency changes. **Root cause of the Recent Bookings horizontal scrollbar**: `RecentBookingsList.tsx`'s scroll container only set `overflow-y-auto`, leaving `overflow-x` at its default `visible` — per the CSS Overflow spec, when one axis is non-`visible` the other axis's `visible` value computes to `auto`, confirmed via `getComputedStyle` showing `overflow-x: auto` despite no such class being applied. The container then had genuine (if minor) horizontal overflow to trigger that scrollbar on: each row uses a `-mx-2` negative margin (paired with its own `px-2`) so the hover background can bleed to the card's edge, but the scroll container itself carries no matching horizontal padding to compensate, so rows measured ~8px wider (`scrollWidth`) than the container's `clientWidth`. Fixed by adding an explicit `overflow-x-hidden` alongside the existing `overflow-y-auto`, which both defeats the CSS-spec auto-coupling and clips the harmless 8px of decorative hover-bleed (no row content — text/icons/dates — was ever within 8px of the edge, confirmed by DOM measurement). Only the container's class string changed; row markup, pagination, and page-size logic are untouched. **QR modal**: `MembershipStatusCard.tsx` gained a `qrModalOpen` state and a "View Full Screen" text button (existing ghost-button convention borrowed from `SportStep.tsx`'s "View Pricing" link: `text-accent-primary underline underline-offset-2 hover:text-accent-dark`) below the existing check-in caption, opening the existing `Modal.tsx` (title-bar/close-X/Escape/backdrop-click/body-scroll-lock all reused unchanged, zero edits to `Modal.tsx`) with only a larger (300×300) QR image plus the same caption text inside — no profile/tier/credit content, confirmed via DOM text-content check on the open modal. **Navbar reorder**: both the desktop button row and the mobile drawer's stacked group now render "Book a Court" before Sign In/Sign Out — a pure JSX reordering, no className/href/onClick changes to either element. VERIFY performed in the Browser pane against the already-running dev server (port 3000, sessionStorage session flag set directly via `javascript_tool` to reach `/account`): scroll container's `getComputedStyle(...).overflowX` confirmed `hidden` (was the implicit `auto` before the fix) on both Recent Bookings pages at 1280px and at 375px, with `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` (no page-level horizontal overflow) at mobile width; QR modal opened via the new button showed only the enlarged image (`naturalWidth`/`naturalHeight` 300×300, loaded) and caption text, with a DOM text-content check confirming no "6-Month"/tier or "credit" substring present inside the dialog; all three close paths (Escape key, the X button, and a synthetic backdrop click) each independently closed the modal, re-verified by reopening between checks; Navbar order confirmed "Book a Court" before "Sign In"/"Sign Out" via `read_page` on `/account` and `/news` (desktop) and via the mobile drawer's DOM child order (`/account`, 375px). Regression: `/login`, `/`, `/book`, `/membership`, `/about`, `/cafe-bar`, `/news` all reloaded with zero console errors. - 2026-08-22 — Relocated the inert "Book a Court" placeholder from `/account`'s standalone sidebar block into the bottom of `MembershipStatusCard.tsx` (full-width card-footer CTA, below the perks/QR row) + **structural fix to the shared `Modal.tsx`, a change affecting every one of its consumers**: the QR "View Full Screen" modal (`MembershipStatusCard.tsx`), the SPORT step pricing modal (`SportStep.tsx`), and three admin modals (`PriceEditModal.tsx`, `ResourceFormModal.tsx`, `BulletinFormModal.tsx`) plus `MembershipReviewActions`' reject form. **Root cause identified for the reported off-center/undimmed rendering**: `Modal.tsx`'s own `flex items-center justify-center` centering classes were already structurally correct — the actual bug was that any Modal instance rendered inside a `Reveal`-wrapped ancestor (e.g. `MembershipStatusCard`, wrapped in `<Reveal>` on `/account`) inherited a `transform: translateY(...)` from Reveal's `translate-y-0`/`translate-y-4` Tailwind classes; per the CSS spec, any ancestor with a `transform` (even `translateY(0)`) creates a new containing block for descendant `position: fixed` elements, so the modal's `fixed inset-0` was positioning itself relative to that transformed ancestor `div` instead of the viewport — reproducing exactly the "anchored near the top" symptom from the reported screenshot. Fixed by rendering the modal through a `createPortal(..., document.body)` in `Modal.tsx` so it always escapes any transformed ancestor, guaranteed to center against the true viewport regardless of where it's mounted in the tree — title bar, close-X, Escape handling, click-outside-to-close, and body-scroll-lock logic are all untouched (same JSX/handlers, just re-parented via the portal). The backdrop's dim was also fixed: it was `bg-brand-dark/40` (the app's brand brown token at 40% opacity — a brand-tinted, barely-there tint, inappropriate for a component shared with the neutral-gray admin panel), changed to `bg-black/60`, a neutral dark tone with materially stronger dimming. Added `overflow-y-auto` and `py-8` (was only `px-4`) to the outer fixed wrapper, plus `my-auto` on the modal card, so a modal taller than a small viewport scrolls within the overlay instead of being clipped, without touching any consumer's own internal scroll handling (e.g. `BulletinFormModal`'s existing `max-h-[70vh] overflow-y-auto` on its form). No Prisma/API/schema/Auth.js change, no new dependency (`react-dom`'s `createPortal` ships with React/Next already). VERIFY performed in the Browser pane against the already-running dev server (port 3000, session flag set via `sessionStorage`/`javascript_tool`): confirmed via DOM query that the standalone sidebar block is gone from `/account` and "Book a Court" now renders inside `MembershipStatusCard`, still a plain `<div>` with no `href`/`onclick` attribute. QR modal measured via `getBoundingClientRect` at both 1280×720 and 375×812 — bounding-box center matched viewport center on both axes at both widths (e.g. 1280px: card center (640, 360) vs. viewport center (640, 360); 375px: card center (187.6, 406) vs. viewport center (187.5, 406)) — and `overlay.parentElement.tagName === 'BODY'` confirmed the portal is escaping `Reveal`'s transformed ancestor. `getComputedStyle` on the backdrop returned `oklab(0 0 0 / 0.6)` (neutral black, 60% alpha) in both cases — not a brand-tinted color. Spot-checked `SportStep`'s "View Pricing" modal on `/book` (not wrapped in a transform, so it never showed the bug, but confirmed the shared component now centers it identically: card center (640, 360) against the same 1280×720 viewport) — pricing content rendered correctly (tier rows + non-member-rate note), no regression. Escape, the close-X button, and a synthetic backdrop click each independently closed the modal (re-verified by reopening between checks). Admin modals (`PriceEditModal`/`ResourceFormModal`/`BulletinFormModal`) were not live-clicked through (would require an `AdminUser` login not set up in this session) but are confirmed via source read to pass through the identical unmodified `<Modal>` wrapper with no divergent JSX — the same portal/centering/backdrop fix applies structurally to all three. Regression: `/login`, `/`, `/book`, `/membership`, `/about`, `/cafe-bar`, `/news` all reloaded with zero console errors. Grepped all three touched files for `dark:`/`border-black`/`bg-foreground`/`text-background`/Prisma imports — zero matches; `package.json` diff empty. No test/DB data involved — no cleanup step needed.

- 2026-08-22 — `/account` Profile-card height-match fix + Recent Bookings scroll-workaround removal, **still hardcoded-sample-data only, no backend**. **Root cause of the Profile/Membership Status height mismatch**: the content grid (`src/app/account/page.tsx`) carried `md:items-start`, which overrides CSS Grid's default `align-items: stretch` and forces each grid item to size to its own content height instead of the shared row's height — since Profile's column and Membership Status's column sit in the same grid row (Recent Bookings, `md:col-span-2`, is alone in the next row and was never affected by this), Profile's column rendered only as tall as `AccountProfile`'s own content while Membership Status's taller content defined the row underneath it, leaving visible empty space. Removing `md:items-start` alone wasn't sufficient, though: even with the grid item itself stretched to the full row height by default `stretch`, that height doesn't automatically reach the actual card element two levels down — the per-column wrapper (`<div className="flex flex-col gap-8">`) is a flex container whose single child sizes to its own content along the main (vertical) axis regardless of the wrapper's own resolved height, so the intervening `Reveal` wrapper and `AccountProfile`'s own root `<div>` both still needed an explicit `h-full` to actually stretch down through that chain. Fixed with three changes: `md:items-start` removed from the grid container; `<Reveal>` wrapping `AccountProfile` given `className="h-full"` (`Reveal.tsx` already forwards a `className` prop onto its own rendered div — zero changes to `Reveal.tsx` itself, satisfying "don't touch Reveal's own animation logic"); `AccountProfile.tsx`'s root `<div>` gained `h-full` alongside its existing classes. `MembershipStatusCard.tsx` and its own `Reveal` wrapper were left completely untouched, since its natural content height is still what defines the row — it doesn't need to stretch into anything. `AccountProfile`'s internal Name/Email/Phone rows were not redistributed or padded; the extra space now simply sits at the bottom of the card, as intended. Separately, `RecentBookingsList.tsx`'s row-list wrapper lost its `h-[600px] overflow-y-auto` (a leftover workaround from the original pagination pass, before pagination guaranteed exactly 10 rows per page) — the wrapper is now just `mt-2 overflow-x-hidden` (the `overflow-x-hidden` half is unrelated and stays: it's the fix from the prior 2026-08-21 pass for the row hover-bleed's genuine ~8px horizontal overflow, still needed independent of any vertical height change). With no explicit height, the container's `overflow-y` computes to `auto` per the CSS spec's non-`visible`-pairing rule (confirmed via `getComputedStyle`), but since the wrapper's height is now `auto` and always exactly matches its 10-row content (`scrollHeight === clientHeight`, confirmed both pages), that computed `auto` never actually triggers a scrollbar — pagination logic, `PAGE_SIZE`, and all row markup are unchanged. No Prisma/API/schema/Auth.js change, no new dependency. VERIFY performed in the Browser pane against the already-running dev server (port 3000, session flag set via `sessionStorage`/`javascript_tool`): at 1280px, `getBoundingClientRect` on both cards' root elements returned identical heights (449.95px each); Recent Bookings' scroll wrapper confirmed `scrollHeight === clientHeight` (667px) with all 10 `dl > div` rows present on page 1, and page 2 (after clicking Next) showed the same — 10 rows, no clipping, `scrollHeight === clientHeight`; the outer Recent Bookings card measured the identical height (823.075px) on both page 1 and page 2. At 375px: cards stack single-column with their own natural (unequal) heights as expected — equal-height was never meant to apply below the `md` breakpoint — Recent Bookings still rendered its full 10-row list with `scrollHeight === clientHeight` and no horizontal overflow (`document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth`, both 375). Regression: `/login`, `/`, `/book`, `/membership`, `/about`, `/cafe-bar`, `/news` all reloaded with zero console errors. Grepped all three touched files for `dark:`/`border-black`/`bg-foreground`/`text-background`/Prisma imports — zero matches; `package.json` diff empty. No test/DB data involved — no cleanup step needed.

Touched the Tech Stack table's Auth row, the Customer & auth model bullet, the Admin/staff model bullet, the API routes coding-convention bullet's "even after Auth.js is decided" phrasing, and removed the now-resolved auth-method bullet from "Open / Not Yet Decided" entirely — the other two bullets there (Member QR code, Member-aware booking flow) are untouched. - 2026-08-22 — Post-approval member activation flow (schema + issuance + email + public activation route/page), deliberately scoped to stop short of real member login (that's Prompt 2 — `/login`/`/account`/`auth.ts`/`auth.config.ts`/`middleware.ts` were not touched, and remain the hardcoded-sample-data scaffold documented in the two 2026-08-21 entries). **Schema**: `Customer` gained a nullable `passwordHash` (`password_hash`, null for every non-member `Customer` row, set only once a member completes activation); a new `MemberActivationToken` model stores a SHA-256 `tokenHash` (never the raw token), `expiresAt`, and a once-only `usedAt`, with a required FK to `Customer`. Hand-written migration (`prisma/migrations/20260822010000_add_member_activation/migration.sql`, applied via `prisma migrate deploy` per the standing shadow-DB workaround, followed by `prisma generate` — which required stopping the locally-running `npm run dev` first, since its held Prisma query-engine DLL was blocking the Windows file rename `prisma generate` needs; the dev server was restarted afterward via the Browser pane's `dev` preview config) enables RLS with `deny_all_anon`/`deny_all_authenticated` policies on the new table in the same migration, matching every other table's convention — `prisma/manual-sql/enable-rls-deny-all.sql` (the master copy) was updated with the same two policies. **Helpers**: new `src/lib/member-activation.ts` (`MEMBER_ACTIVATION_TOKEN_HOURS` env var, default 48 — same "plain env var, not DB-driven" pattern as `BOOKING_HOLD_MINUTES` — plus `generateActivationToken()`/`hashActivationToken()`, SHA-256 over a 256-bit `crypto.randomBytes` token, no salt needed since the input is already high-entropy) and new `src/lib/resend.ts` (`sendActivationEmail`, a plain `fetch()` POST to `https://api.resend.com/emails` — no `resend` npm package installed, matching `supabase-storage.ts`'s no-new-SDK convention — wrapped in try/catch that only logs on failure and never throws, mirroring `paymongo.ts`'s `expirePaymongoCheckoutSession` best-effort pattern exactly; sender is `onboarding@resend.dev`, Resend's sandbox default, since the project has no verified sending domain yet). Member password hashing/verification reuses the existing `hashPassword`/`verifyPassword` from `src/lib/admin-auth.ts` unchanged (one-line comments at both import sites note these are generic, not admin-specific, despite the file's name) — no duplicated crypto logic. **Approval mutation** (`src/app/api/admin/memberships/[id]/route.ts`): the approve branch's initial `findUnique` now `include`s `customer`; after the existing membership-creation `$transaction` commits, a customer with no `passwordHash` gets a `MemberActivationToken` row (created outside that transaction, same "side effects only after the core transaction commits" pattern this file already used) and a best-effort activation email with a `${NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL}/activate?token=...` link; a customer who already has a `passwordHash` (a returning/renewing member) gets neither — this is expected, not an error. The response gained one additive `activationEmailSent: boolean` field; every existing field is unchanged. **Public activation flow**: `POST /api/activate` (public, no auth, same trust tier as membership application submission) validates `token`/`password`/`confirmPassword` (400 on missing fields, password under 8 characters, or a mismatch), hashes the incoming token and looks it up by `tokenHash` (404 `'Invalid activation link'` if not found), and returns specific 400s for an already-used or expired token before setting `Customer.passwordHash` and stamping `usedAt` in a single `$transaction`. New `/activate` page (`src/app/activate/page.tsx`, a server component wrapping `<Suspense>`, same pattern as `book/confirmation/page.tsx`) + co-located `ActivateForm.tsx` (client component, styled on the same brand-token/form conventions as `MembershipApplicationForm.tsx` — `bg-brand-light`/`border-brand-dark/20`/`accent-primary` inputs and button, not the older `zinc`/`dark:` styling `book/confirmation` happens to use) reads `token` from `useSearchParams()`, shows a no-fetch "missing token" state when absent, and otherwise renders the password/confirm-password form, branching on response status exactly per the established convention (400/404 surface `json.error` inline with form state preserved, network/other failures show a generic message) with a "Your account is activated" success state linking to `/login`. **VERIFY**: `pg_class.relrowsecurity`/`pg_policies` confirmed RLS + both deny-all policies on `member_activation_tokens`; `information_schema.columns` confirmed `customers.password_hash` exists and is nullable; `booking_no_overlap` and the other 16 tables' RLS reconfirmed unchanged. A disposable Prisma script (`prisma/verify-activation.ts`, deleted after running) exercised the approve branch's actual token-issuance logic end-to-end: a throwaway no-password `Customer` + pending `MembershipApplication` produced a `MemberActivationToken` with a future `expiresAt`, null `usedAt`, and a 64-char-hex `tokenHash` that round-tripped correctly through `hashActivationToken(rawToken)`; a second throwaway `Customer` with an existing `passwordHash` produced zero token rows; `hashPassword`/`verifyPassword` round-tripped correctly on a test password. `sendActivationEmail` was verified two ways without sending a real email (dispatching one is an externally-visible send needing its own explicit go-ahead, which wasn't sought here): a mocked-`fetch` script (`prisma/verify-resend.ts`, deleted after running) confirmed the request URL, `Authorization: Bearer ...` header, `from: onboarding@resend.dev`, and HTML body are all constructed correctly; a separate real call against the live Resend API with a deliberately malformed `RESEND_API_KEY` came back a genuine 401, caught and logged, never thrown. `POST /api/activate` was exercised via direct `curl` calls (not a browser form-fill, consistent with every prior admin-mutation VERIFY in this project) against a disposable valid token and a disposable pre-expired token (`prisma/verify-activate-setup.ts` / `verify-activate-check-and-cleanup.ts`, both deleted after running): fabricated token → 404 `'Invalid activation link'`; mismatched passwords → 400 `'Passwords do not match'`; a 5-character password → 400 `'Password must be at least 8 characters'`; the pre-expired token → 400 `'This activation link has expired'`; the valid token's first use → 200, confirmed via a follow-up query that `Customer.passwordHash` was set and the token's `usedAt` was stamped; a present token renders the form; success and error (`'already been used'`) states were both confirmed by mocking `window.fetch` before submitting a dummy (non-real) password — per this VERIFY pass's own constraint against typing a real password into a rendered activation/login form — rather than submitting against the real endpoint.
